# CGHL rulebook audit, 27 September 2026

Rulebook version audited: 3.49. Audited against the live database (412 functions, the triggers, row-level security and 13 scheduled jobs), the site code, the Netlify functions, the Discord bot and the season settings. Read-only throughout: nothing was changed.

## How it was done

1. Every paragraph of the book, including the dormant full-format Appendix A text, was checked by one auditor per chapter: 294 paragraphs, 763 separate claims.
2. Every claim that did not match (335) went to an adversarial verifier told to refute it. 294 held; 37 were overturned or corrected, 20 of them back to a match.
3. Nine sweeps listed what the system enforces that the book never states (419 behaviors, 311 of them not fully in the book), and three cross-cutting passes checked the book against itself, the site's own copy, and the full-format appendix (90 findings).
4. Each chapter was consolidated into fixes and questions. Every item that rested on a single agent's check (86 questions, 113 fixes) then went to a second independent checker: 153 confirmed, 46 corrected, none refuted. 19 questions turned out to be settled by the book already and are listed as fixes instead.
5. I re-verified the most serious findings myself against the live database before writing this.

**Result:** of 763 claims, 428 match the system exactly (56%). The rest: 110 partly enforced, 36 contradicted, 12 unimplemented, 30 stale, 41 ambiguous, 69 done by hand by the office, 37 conduct standards no code can check.

## Part 1. Live defects: the book is right and the system is wrong

These need no decision from you, only a go-ahead. Each one I confirmed myself against the live database.

**1. SECURITY: anyone can post into any club's private Discord room.** public.club_notify, public._post_lineup_notice, public._notify_lineup_pulled and public._notify_lineup_moves run with elevated rights, are executable by anonymous visitors, and check no caller. With the public site key, anyone could post arbitrary text into any club's room, ping the club role, and ring the Owner, GM and AGM notification bells, and name any role to ping. Nothing on the site, in the Netlify functions or in the bot calls them through the API. tools/sql/grant-audit.sql missed them: it treats any mention of auth.uid() as a guard (club_notify uses it only as a default for the actor field) and does not follow functions that write through another function.
*Fix:* Revoke EXECUTE from public, anon and authenticated on all four; teach grant-audit.sql both blind spots.

**2. Rule 1.1.5, the Discord-departure sweep, has not run since 11:49 PM ET on September 25.** One camp player was noticed on September 24, rejoined on the 25th and left again. Each sweep now tries to insert a second notice for him, hits the primary key and rolls back the entire sweep, so no club has been noticed and no removal has happened for anyone since. Three rostered players have left the Discord in that time (a Dallas camp player, a Pittsburgh camp player who has since returned, and a Utah active-roster player) and none of their clubs was told; two are still out. The sync reports ok:false on every tick.
*Fix:* Reopen an existing notice instead of inserting a second one (a fresh 24-hour window from the new notice), and close notices whose player no longer holds a spot. Then let the sweep catch up.

**3. Rules 5.2.1, 7.2.2 and 8.3.4: a suspended player can be dressed.** set_game_lineup checks only whether the manager filing the sheet is suspended, never the six players on it. game_lineups has no triggers; the suspension trigger sits on the legacy lineups table, which is empty. Only the browser grays a suspended player out, and review_game_records never checks suspensions, so a suspended player who plays is never reported.
*Fix:* Refuse a suspended or banned player in set_game_lineup and the line setters; pull a newly suspended player from lineups already filed and tell his club; add a suspended-player finding to the post-game review.

**4. Rule 7.2.2: a suspension never ends on its own.** suspensions.status can only be 'active' or 'lifted'. When a games or dated suspension is served, the database stops enforcing it, but every screen still shows the member as suspended, the lineup builder still refuses him, and the forum guard still blocks his posts, until an official lifts it by hand. Unserved games at season end never finish at all.
*Fix:* Mark a suspension served when it is served, mirror that on the site, and point the forum guard at is_suspended. How games carry over is question 5 below.

**5. Rules 7.2.1 and 7.7.2: staff can sanction staff.** Both disciplinary functions refuse only a target whose role is commissioner. Any officiating or community official can warn or suspend any of the eight staff members; the book says only a commissioner may.
*Fix:* Extend the refusal to targets with the staff role.

**6. Rule 7.7 (v3.39): the community 3, 6 or 9-game scale can be bypassed.** The scale and the fixed headings are enforced on the Moderation card, but a community moderator ruling from a case goes through discipline_from_case, which still allows any 1 to 10 games or up to 30 days, in free text, with no headings recorded. This is a gap in what I shipped on September 26.
*Fix:* Give discipline_from_case the same community branch and a headings parameter.

**7. Rule 2.3.1: either club can rewrite a trade's status directly.** Row-level security lets any Owner, GM or AGM of either club update a trade row, and the status column is writable. No trigger checks transitions, so a seat can mark an offer accepted without a player moving (both rooms are then told the trade is complete), mark it reversed, revive a declined offer, or relabel a completed trade so reverse_trade refuses it.
*Fix:* Guard status transitions: declined only by the receiver, withdrawn only by the proposer, accepted only through accept_trade, reversed only through reverse_trade, nothing out of accepted or reversed.

**8. Rule 3.2.4: a staffer can lift a forfeit in his own club's game.** undo_forfeit and unforfeit_game check the caller's desk only. Four of four officiating staff, three of three commissioners and one statistics staffer hold roster spots.
*Fix:* Refuse when the caller is rostered on, or holds a seat with, either club in the game. (Question 21 asks whether the same bar covers every game ruling.)

**9. Rules 4.2.3 and 8.3.2: playoff games get no box-score review.** review_game_records returns immediately for any stage other than regular, so from November 4 no unrostered, unidentified or out-of-position player is reported in a playoff game. Only the series cap is checked.
*Fix:* Run the review for playoff games, routed to officiating, before November 4.

**10. Rule 4.3.7: the playoff series counter ignores forfeits.** trg_conclude_series and check_playoff_clinches count wins by goals only, so a Rule 4.3 forfeit in which the forfeiting club scored more would count as its win when deciding a series or a clinch, and a forfeit ruled after the final never re-runs the count.
*Fix:* Decide each game by forfeit first, goals second, and re-run the series on a forfeit change. Before November 4.

**11. Rule 4.3.9: the automatic abandoned-game ruling can no longer fire.** You ordered it on September 23 (v2.94). Since v3.18 a short sitting is held as 'scheduled', but the abandonment sweep reads only finals, so it has never filed one. The held-game notice then steers statistics staff to the Rule 3.2 forfeit button, which deletes both clubs' statistics, the opposite of 4.3.9.
*Fix:* Make the sweep read held games, give staff a single abandoned-game door that keeps the statistics, and fix the notice.

**12. Rules 2.2.3 and 2.5.2 (v3.46): the waive dialog still says 'at the league minimum'.** The live waive confirmation (src/live/part6_hub.js:2358), the Team HQ roster footer and the posted waiver guide still say a waived player is signed at the league minimum, and the guide still says a player must play 3 games before a waiver. v3.46 made the salary carry and v3.41 removed the minimum. This is copy I missed on September 26.
*Fix:* Correct the dialog, footer and club-room notice; rebuild and repost the waiver guide.

**13. Rule 5.3: the posted lineup guide teaches the abolished penalty rule.** The management lineup guide still says a change after the lock is an emergency call-up that costs an in-game penalty per player. Rule 5.3 has said since v3.05 that it costs nothing until ten minutes after puck drop. An opponent reading the old guide may demand penalties the book abolished.
*Fix:* Rebuild and repost the lineup guide.

**14. Rule 0.5.1: the season editor's Auto-space sets the draft for 7:00 PM.** Both branches of Auto-space write 19:00 ET; the book, the draft room and both season rows say 9:00 PM. Running Auto-space and saving would move a draft to 7 PM.
*Fix:* Write 21:00 ET, and change Appendix A's 7:00 PM to 9:00 PM.

## Part 2. Questions for you

For each question, **Today** is what the system does now. If you are happy with today's behavior, say so and I will write the book to match. 19 other questions the auditors raised are already answered by the book; they are in Part 4 as fixes.

### Decide soon: these affect games, rosters or discipline now

**Q1.** (Q-2-1) **When a player is waived, does he become a free agent at once (Rule 2.2.3), or is there a twenty-four hour waiver period with claims (Rule 2.5.4)?** Rule 2.5.

- *Why it matters:* The two paragraphs contradict each other on who may take a waived player, when, and in what order; managers act on whichever they read.
- *Options:* (a) Free agent at once, any club with room may sign him immediately at the salary he carries (TODAY). Book: rewrite 2.5.4 to 'Waiving a player removes his cap hit from the club immediately. There is no waiver period and no claim: the player becomes a free agent at once under Rule 2.2 and may be signed by any club at the salary he carried until the movement deadline (Rule 2.4).', change 'claim a waived player' in 2.5.1 to 'sign a waived player', and fix 2.5.full.4 alike. (b) Build a 24-hour claim period with a claiming order (for example worst record first) before he reaches the pool; 2.2.3 is then rewritten.
- *Today:* Option (a): waive_player stamps waived_at and sign_free_agent signs immediately; no claim mechanism exists.

**Q2.** (Q-2-2) **To sign a waived player, must a club have room in his position group on the active roster, or only fewer than fifteen active players?** Rule 2.2.

- *Why it matters:* Decides whether a club whose forward group is full may still sign a forward (into camp), and whether a club with fifteen active players can sign anyone although camp is unlimited.
- *Options:* (a) Active total under the published figure; if his group is full he joins training camp (TODAY). Book 2.2.3 and 2.5.4 become: 'any club whose active roster holds fewer players than the published total may sign a waived player; where his position group is already full he joins the club's training camp.' (b) Room in his position group (the book): sign_free_agent gains a group test.
- *Today:* Option (a): sign_free_agent tests only pro_roster_count >= roster_max; place_new_roster_spot parks him in camp.

**Q3.** (Q-9-10-5) **Does 'dress' mean named on the filed lineup, or actually appearing in the game lobby?** Rule 10.1.1.

- *Why it matters:* The forfeiture rules of 5.2.1 and 8.3.3 turn on 'dressing' an ineligible player. Under the lobby reading an over-cap or out-of-position box-score appearance forfeits automatically; under the lineup reading it goes to the officials under Rule 5.3.
- *Options:* A: 'dress' means named on the filed lineup for a game, which is how 5.1.3, 5.3.3 and the site's labels use it. Book: redefine in 10.1.1 ("'dress' means to name a player on a club's filed lineup for a game") and add "'appear' means to take part in a game in a league lobby, as shown by the box score"; reword 5.2.2 so the weekly cap counts appearances for played games and filed lineups for games not yet played. System: no change. B: keep the lobby meaning. Book: reword 5.1.3, 5.3.3 and 5.2.2 to 'name on the lineup'. System: an ineligible box-score appearance must forfeit the game automatically under 5.2.1 and 8.3.3, which it does not do today.
- *Today:* Eligibility is enforced only on the filed sheet: set_game_lineup refuses a player without an active contract with the club, a player past his weekly or series cap (player_week_games) and a playoff player under the games floor. It does NOT refuse a suspended player (the suspension trigger sits on the empty legacy lineups table, not on game_lineups). Nothing forfeits a game automatically for an ineligible player, filed or appearing; review_game_records only reports box-score appearances to Officiating.

**Q4.** (Q-7-5) **What does a suspension take from a member who holds a club seat or a league-office position: all of his front-office (and official) powers, only some, or only his playing and point-of-contact duties as Rule 7.2.2 reads?** Rule 7.2.2.

- *Why it matters:* A club whose GM is suspended for a Discord outburst cannot set its lineups unless another seat acts, yet he can still make draft picks and call-ups; a suspended official can still suspend others. None of this is in the book.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: he keeps his seat but may not set lineups or lines, change jersey numbers, veto a server, waive, sign or offer, extend, answer offers, trade, list on the trade block or queue management moves; he may still call up and send down, draft, and use the game stats desk; game-night point-of-contact duties are not checked; he may not post on the forums; a suspended official keeps every league-office power. (b) Everything: add guards to set_roster_squad, swap_roster_squad, draft_make_pick, the game stats desk and the game-night functions, and to the discipline and case tools for a suspended official. Book: add to 7.2.2 "A suspended member who holds a club seat keeps the seat but may exercise none of its powers while the suspension runs, and the club's other managers act in his place; a suspended official may exercise no power of the league office; a suspended member may read but not post on the league forums." (c) Only what 7.2.2 says: remove the actor guards and keep the playing and point-of-contact bar.
- *Today:* A suspended seat holder keeps his seat. The database refuses him: lineups and lines (set_game_lineup, clear_game_lineup, set_team_line*), jersey numbers, server vetoes (RLS game_vetoes_write), waive, sign and offer, extensions, contract-offer writes and trades (block_suspended_actor on contract_offers and trades), direct roster_spots updates including the trade block (RLS), and queued management moves (mgmt_request_move). He may still call up and send down (set_roster_squad, swap_roster_squad), draft (draft_make_pick) and use the lag-out stats desk (ingest-stats.js). Game-night point-of-contact functions are unchecked. He may not post on the forums (_forum_guard), and that bar lasts until the row is lifted, even after the suspension is served. A suspended official keeps every league-office power. The dress bar itself is NOT enforced on the live per-game lineup: set_game_lineup never checks the dressed players, and block_suspended_lineup guards only the unused lineups table.

**Q5.** (Q-7-6) **Which games count toward a suspension measured in games: the finals of the club he was on when suspended in that season only (today), or the games of whichever club he is on, carried into the next season?** Rule 7.2.2.

- *Why it matters:* Today unserved games at a season's end never count down again, so the member stays suspended in every later season until lifted by hand, and a traded player serves on his former club's games. Rule 7.2.2 promises games carry over, which implies they are served.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: every final of the club at issue, from its count at issue, in the season of issue (forfeits and playoff games included); frozen at season end; follows the old club after a trade or waiver. (b) Games of the club he is on at each game, carried into the next season on the first games of the club with which he next appears. System reworks is_suspended. Book: add to 7.2.2 "A suspension measured in games is served one game for each official result of the club on whose roster the player stands, whether or not he would have dressed; forfeited and playoff games count. Games unserved at the end of a season are served on the first games of the club with which he next appears." (c) Keep (a) within the season; at rollover the league office converts the balance by hand, and the book says so.
- *Today:* suspend_player and discipline_from_case freeze team_id at issue (the player's active roster spot in current_season_id()) along with start_game_count = team_final_count(team, season). public.is_suspended(uuid) counts that club's finals in that season, all stages, forfeits included. It does not follow the player after a trade or waiver. At season end the count stops moving, so a suspension with unserved games never finishes: is_suspended stays TRUE indefinitely until someone lifts it by hand. flip_season_status auto-lifts only 'seasons' mode. In effect the suspension 'carries' but can never be served.

**Q6.** (Q-7-7) **When a member already under suspension is suspended again, do the two run one after the other, or does the new ruling replace the old?** Rule 7.2.1.

- *Why it matters:* Today the new ruling silently wipes the unserved balance of the old one (and his active warnings), which can shorten the total sanction and leaves the member unnotified of the change.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: replacement; prior active suspensions (and, from the Moderation card, warnings) are lifted without notice. Book: add to 7.2.1 "A suspension imposed on a member already under suspension supersedes the earlier one; the official imposing it sets its length to account for any games or days left unserved." System should at least stop closing warnings. (b) Consecutive: the new suspension starts when the old one is served. System: both RPCs stop lifting the prior row, and the countdown starts after it. Book: "Suspensions imposed for separate conduct are served consecutively."
- *Today:* Option (a) (suspend_player and discipline_from_case update prior active rows to 'lifted').

**Q7.** (Q-7-8) **How is a member who is not on a roster (a free agent, a registrant, or a manager with a seat but no roster spot) suspended in games, and in particular for Discord conduct?** Rule 7.4.3, 7.7.2.

- *Why it matters:* Today such a member can be suspended only until a date, and a community-only moderator, who may not use dates, cannot suspend him at all, although Rule 7.7 covers every member in league spaces.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: dates only, by officiating or a commissioner; community must refer it. Book: add to 7.7.2 "A member who is not on a club roster is suspended by the officiating department or a commissioner, for a stated period." (b) A fixed number of days per step of the 3/6/9 scale (the commissioner sets the number), usable by community; system allows date mode on that ladder. (c) The games count against the league schedule (every league night) rather than a club's finals; system adds that mode.
- *Today:* Both suspend_player and discipline_from_case refuse games mode unless the member holds an active roster_spots row in current_season_id(). So a free agent, a registrant, or a manager with a seat and no roster spot can only be suspended by date (or, on the case path, through a season). The claim that community cannot use dates is only half true. suspend_player, the conduct modal, does refuse date mode for a community-only moderator. discipline_from_case, reached from the 'Issue discipline' button on any case card (part_live.js:8194, 8380 to 8420), lets community issue a date suspension of up to 30 days, or 1 to 10 games, under the general staff ceiling and not the 3/6/9 ladder. So community can suspend an off-roster member by date today, through a case.

**Q8.** (Q-7-9) **What happens when a suspended player appears in a league game anyway: a flag for the officials to rule on case by case, or a fixed consequence for the club and the player?** Rule 7.2.2.

- *Why it matters:* Nothing happens today: the game stands and no one is told. F-7-9 adds the flag; the book must say what follows it.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: nothing, the result stands. (b) The importer flags it to the Officials desk, who rule under Rule 7.2.1. Book: add to 7.2.2 "A club that dresses a suspended player is subject to discipline under this chapter." (c) Automatic: the club forfeits the game and the suspension is extended by a set number of games. Book states both numbers; system applies them.
- *Today:* Option (a) (review_game_records has no suspension check).

**Q9.** (Q-7-2) **Who decides an appeal, in particular the appeal of a community (Discord conduct) suspension: one official other than the one who imposed it, a panel of several, or a commissioner?** Rule 7.1.2, 7.6.2.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 7.6.2 promises a panel; Rule 7.1.2 sends every appeal to officiating while saying officiating should not decide chat matters. Neither is how appeals work today.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: every appeal is announced to officiating and any one non-media official or commissioner may decide it, including the official who issued the sanction. (b) One official, not the issuer, from the department that issued the sanction (a community appeal heard by another community moderator). Book: 7.6.2 reads "Appeals are decided by an official other than the one who imposed the original sanction"; 7.1.2 routes each appeal to the issuing department. System: route by the linked sanction and refuse the issuer (F-7-7). (c) A panel of two or more officials excluding the issuer, with community appeals heard by a commissioner. Book as today; system needs a panel vote like application ballots.
- *Today:* Option (a), with no issuer exclusion (bot/staff-alerts.mjs appeal to officiating; RLS ar_update).

**Q10.** (Q-7-12) **Should a ban placed directly in Discord by a moderator stand, or is a ban recorded on the website the only way to keep a linked member out of the Discord?** Rule 7.2.1, 7.7.

- *Why it matters:* Today any Discord ban on a linked member who is not banned on the site is lifted automatically within minutes, so a moderator's emergency ban silently undoes itself.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: the site is the only record; discord-sync lifts every guild ban on a linked, unbanned member. Book: add to 7.2.1 "Removal from the league Discord is made only by an expulsion recorded on the website by a commissioner; a ban placed in Discord by other means is lifted automatically." (b) Discord bans stand: the sync lifts only bans the site itself placed (tracked through ban_events), and the book says community moderators may remove a member from the Discord pending a commissioner ruling.
- *Today:* Option (a) (netlify/functions/discord-sync.js:1742 to 1747).

**Q11.** (Q-1-5) **After the movement deadline, when a player is removed for leaving the Discord (or withdrawn by the league office), may his club fill the spot, or does it play short through the playoffs?** Rule 1.1.5 / 2.4.1.

- *Why it matters:* The club is told 'the spot is free', but after the deadline every signing is refused and no late registrant is placed, so a club can lose a player it has no way to replace, during the playoffs included.
- *Options:* (a) Removals continue after the deadline and the club plays short (WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES TODAY, apart from the wording of the club notice). Book: 1.1.5 adds 'This procedure applies throughout the season, the playoffs included; after the movement deadline the spot is not filled (Rule 2.4).' System: the post-deadline club notice should stop saying the spot is free to fill. (b) Removals continue and the club may sign one waived free agent in the vacated position group as an exception to Rule 2.4. System: a narrow bypass of guard_roster_waive for that signing; book: exception written into 1.1.5 and 2.4.1. (c) No automatic removal after the deadline; the office is told and rules on it. System: sweep skips seasons where moves_locked(); book: 1.1.5 says so.
- *Today:* public.sweep_roster_departures(integer) covers every roster spot in a season whose status is not 'complete', so it runs through the playoffs. It sets app.office_removal='1', and public.guard_roster_waive() then lets the delete through even after moves_locked(). Its final club notice says 'His sign-up is revoked and the spot is free.' public.office_withdraw_registration(uuid,text) uses the same bypass, but its club notice says only that the player 'comes off your squad', without saying the spot is free. After the deadline a club cannot sign a replacement: public.sign_free_agent raises 'Roster moves are locked' unless the caller is a commissioner, and the caller must also hold a club management seat. The club can still fill an active-roster hole by calling up from its own training camp, which Rule 2.4.2 exempts from the deadline (subject to the weekly freeze), so it does not necessarily play short.

**Q12.** (Q-1-6) **When the league office removes a player from a club's roster (Control Center 'remove from roster'), is he a free agent any club may sign, a registrant to be placed again, or out of the season?** Rule 1.1.3 / 2.2.

- *Why it matters:* The tool resets the registration to 'pending' without a waived_at stamp, so he is not on the waived players board; a player who registered after the cutoff is re-placed on a random club within five minutes, while a drafted or pre-cutoff player sits with no club and no route back. The log calls it a waive.
- *Options:* (a) Free agent: system stamps waived_at so he appears on the waived board and keeps his salary as Rule 2.2.3 describes; book adds 'A player removed by the league office becomes a free agent under Rule 2.2.3.' (b) Placed again: system routes him through _assign_reg_random regardless of registration time; book adds 'is placed again under Rule 2.8.' (c) Out of the season: system uses office_withdraw_registration instead; book adds 'takes no further part in the season.' Today the result depends on when he registered: post-cutoff registrants get (b), everyone else gets none of the three.
- *Today:* public.admin_remove_from_roster(uuid,uuid) is commissioner-only. It deletes the roster spot, which fires contract_on_waive and expires the contract, then sets the registration to status 'pending' without stamping waived_at, and writes a 'waive' transaction 'League office removed X from CLUB'. The UI (part_live.js:6438) tells the commissioner that it 'waives the player back to the free-agent pool'. In fact, before the deadline, the pg_cron job rookie-distribution (every 2 min, public.distribute_unproven_rookies) picks up ANY non-waived, unrostered, uncontracted, unsuspended registration, drafted or not and whatever its registration time. It re-places him at random through _assign_reg_random as depth in the training camp of the club with the fewest players, possibly his old club, within minutes. auto_assign_latecomers (post-cutoff, never drafted) is a second path. After moves_locked() both sweeps stop, so he stays unplaced. A drafted player then shows as 'Free agent' on the board because CG.poolState falls back to isReturning, but sign_free_agent is locked for clubs. A never-drafted player shows as 'Awaiting placement'.

**Q13.** (Q-1-1) **When a waived free agent (placed once, now on no roster) has been out of the Discord for a day, should his registration be withdrawn automatically like an unplaced sign-up, or should he stay a free agent and be left to the league office?** Rule 1.1.4 / 2.2.3.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.2.3 promises that nothing happens to a waived player automatically and that any club may sign him until the deadline; Rule 1.1.4 covers only players 'before being placed'. The system withdraws him, and did so twice on 2026-09-27, once two seconds after his club waived him.
- *Options:* (a) Withdraw automatically after a day out of the server (WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES TODAY). Book: 1.1.4 reads 'A registered player who leaves the league Discord while not on a club roster or in a training camp, including a waived free agent, has his sign-up withdrawn automatically ...', and 2.2.3 adds 'unless his sign-up is withdrawn under Rule 1.1.4'. (b) Keep him a free agent; the office rules on him. System: remove_departed_signups must exclude waived_at IS NOT NULL and flag the player to the operations desk; book: 1.1.4 adds 'A waived free agent who leaves the Discord is referred to the league office, which rules on his registration.'
- *Today:* Option (a): remove_departed_signups withdraws any pending registration with no roster spot, waived or not.

**Q14.** (Q-5-2) **Does the weekly availability duty of Rule 5.1 bind training-camp players, or only players on the active roster?** Rule 5.1.

- *Why it matters:* Camp players are reminded, pinged, nudged by DM and named to their clubs today, and would be reached by any consecutive-miss review (Q-5-3); Rule 5.2.3 uses 'rostered' to mean active roster as opposed to camp, and 'rostered player' is undefined in 10.1.
- *Options:* (a) Both active roster and camp (WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES TODAY): Rule 5.1.1 reads 'Every player on a club's active roster or in its training camp', and 10.1 defines 'rostered player' accordingly. (b) Active roster only: public.availability_missing gains a squad filter (squad <> 'tc'), the 24-hour reminder and nudges stop naming camp players, and Rule 5.1.1 reads 'Every active-roster player'.
- *Today:* public.availability_missing selects every roster_spots row with status 'active' and no squad filter; 56 of 170 Season 1 spots are camp spots and are treated the same.

**Q15.** (Q-5-3) **When a player submits no availability in consecutive weeks, should the system flag him automatically to a staff desk (after how many weeks, and with which outcomes: warning, release to the free-agent pool, suspension), or is the review left to the office's own observation?** Rule 5.1.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 5.1.4 and the nudge DM both promise a review that nothing surfaces; the first possible two-week miss exists when Week 2 closes on Wednesday, September 30.
- *Options:* (a) Build it: two consecutive missed weeks raise a flag on a staff desk, a staffer releases or warns with one click, and Rule 5.1.4 states 'A player who submits no availability in two consecutive game weeks is referred automatically to the league office, which may warn him or release him from his club.' (b) Leave it manual: Rule 5.1.4 says 'may be reviewed by the league office' and the nudge DM's 'puts you under league-office review' is softened to match. TODAY neither: nothing tracks consecutive misses, only the availability_nudges ledger exists, and the DM promises review.
- *Today:* No escalation exists (memory/inactivity-ledger-plan.md step 3 NOT built); the DM sent by availability_nudge_tick says two weeks in a row puts the player under league-office review.

**Q16.** (Q-5-4) **Must a filed lineup name all six players with a player rostered as a goaltender in goal, or may a club file an incomplete sheet and, while the position lock is lifted, put any player in goal?** Rule 5.2.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 5.2.1 requires 'a rostered goaltender' while Rule 5.2.4 lets any player play any position with the lock off; an incomplete sheet is what the opponent sees at the lock and counts against the named players' weeks.
- *Options:* (a) Incomplete sheets allowed and any player may play goal while the lock is lifted (WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES TODAY: 14 of 83 Season 1 sheets are incomplete, 3 have no goaltender): Rule 5.2.1 reads 'including a player dressed in goal' and adds 'A filed lineup may be incomplete; it is the lineup that takes the ice that must be eligible.' (b) The database refuses a sheet with an empty slot (or at least an empty goal) and requires a rostered goaltender in goal regardless of the lock: set_game_lineup changes, clear_lineups_on_roster_remove must withdraw an affected sheet instead of blanking one slot, and Rule 5.2.4 gains a goaltender exception.
- *Today:* set_game_lineup accepts null slots; lineup_slot_ok skips all group tests while position_locks is false; the roster-removal trigger blanks the departed player's slot.

**Q17.** (Q-3-4) **Can a club that is short of players get a reschedule if it asks in advance and the other club agrees in writing?** Rule 3.2.5, 3.2.6, 3.3.1 (and 5.1.2).

- *Why it matters:* Rule 3.2.5 says a one-sided failure is never a reschedule; Rules 3.2.6 and 5.1.2 tell a short-handed club to pursue one, and Rule 3.3.1 allows any agreed move. A club cannot tell whether asking is allowed.
- *Options:* A (TODAY in practice: the tool applies no condition): yes. Rule 3.2.5 governs only failures at game time; reword it: 'At game time, a one-sided failure is a forfeit, not a reschedule; a game-time reschedule is available only when both clubs have a genuine connection problem.' Rules 3.2.6, 3.3.1 and 5.1.2 stand. B: no. A reschedule always requires a genuine problem shared by both clubs or a league-wide event; strike the reschedule instruction from Rules 3.2.6 and 5.1.2 and add the condition to Rule 3.3.1.
- *Today:* public.ops_reschedule_game moves any unfinished game for operations staff or a commissioner with no condition on why.

**Q18.** (Q-4-10) **Should a combined game be published as a result only once its sittings reach a full game (or it was decided in overtime), as a single sitting is?** Rule 4.3.

- *Why it matters:* A single short sitting is held unpublished, but once a second sitting merges the game is set final even if the combined clock is still short, so an incomplete game can enter the standings.
- *Options:* A: hold a combined game until it reaches a full game or overtime, as with one sitting; the importer changes and the hold is written into 4.3.3 or 6.2.1. B: publish on any merge, as today; 4.3.3 says a combined game is published when the reload is combined, whatever its length.
- *Today:* B: ingestContinuation and leagueMerge set status 'final' unconditionally.

**Q19.** (Q-4-5) **When a game is restarted under Rule 4.5 because of an illegal loadout, do the goals and statistics from the abandoned sitting count?** Rule 4.5.

- *Why it matters:* The importer treats a short sitting followed by a replay between the same clubs as one game and adds the sittings together, so goals scored with the banned build on the ice enter the official box score.
- *Options:* A: the restart is a fresh game and the abandoned sitting is struck; the book says so and the statistics desk removes that sitting (a strike tool may be needed). B: as with a Rule 4.3 replay, the sittings combine and everything counts; the book says so. Today the system does B automatically, while the book reads like A.
- *Today:* B: ingest-stats.js merges the sittings and adds their goals and statistics.

**Q20.** (Q-4-3) **When a club disconnects during overtime, do the clubs replay one period played as overtime (sudden death, penalty as soon as possible after puck drop) or replay the full game?** Rule 4.3.

- *Why it matters:* The incident desk lets staff log an overtime disconnection, but neither the book nor the ruling text posted to the clubs says how it is replayed or where the penalty is taken.
- *Options:* A: one period played as sudden-death overtime, penalty as soon as possible after puck drop (the 4.3.6 model); adds a sentence to 4.3.6 and an overtime branch to log_game_incident. B: a full replay with the penalty taken within one minute of the disconnection time (the 4.3.2 model); adds a sentence to 4.3.2. Today: neither is stated; the ruling text for period 4 carries no timing guidance.
- *Today:* The staff incident form offers an OT period (src/live/part_live.js:2740, option value 4). public.log_game_incident has branches only for early third and period 3, so an OT disconnection gets the generic 4.3.2 text: 'One penalty on the reload, within one minute of the game-clock time of the disconnection' (and the same default in bot/incidents.mjs timingNote). So the system does give timing guidance: the full-replay 4.3.2 model, applied by default.

**Q21.** (Q-3-5) **Should the bar on ruling a matter about oneself (Rule 7.1.2) extend to every game ruling: logging an incident, entering or reversing a forfeit, and correcting a result?** Rule 3.2 (new 3.2.7), 7.1.2.

- *Why it matters:* Every commissioner and every officiating staffer is rostered this season. Today a staffer can enter a forfeit against his own opponent or correct the score of his own game, and nothing refuses it; the book bars conflicts only for the forfeit waiver (3.2.4) and for cases (7.1.2).
- *Options:* A: extend it. Add Rule 3.2.7: 'No member of league staff may log an incident, enter or reverse a forfeit, or correct the result of a game in which he plays, or which involves a club he manages.' Add the F-3-1 guard to declare_forfeit, forfeit_game, log_game_incident and stats_game_set_result as well. B: keep the bar to the waiver only (F-3-1), and rely on the audit log for the rest. TODAY: no game ruling checks involvement, not even the waiver.
- *Today:* declare_forfeit, forfeit_game, undo_forfeit, unforfeit_game, log_game_incident and stats_game_set_result check desk only.

**Q22.** (Q-3-2) **May the officiating department void a game that neither club played, or only a commissioner?** Rule 3.2.5.

- *Why it matters:* The book reserves the void to the commissioner; the database lets any officiating staffer void a game and lift a void.
- *Options:* A (TODAY): officiating or a commissioner may void. Book change to Rule 3.2.5: 'the league office voids it' (or 'the officiating department or a commissioner voids it'). B: commissioner only. Narrow the void branch of declare_forfeit, and the void half of undo_forfeit, to is_commissioner(); the book stands.
- *Today:* public.declare_forfeit and public.undo_forfeit admit a commissioner or the officiating department for both forfeits and voids.

**Q23.** (Q-6-3) **Who may rebuild a lagged-out game from the club game stats desk: either club's management, or only the winning club's?** Rule 6.2.1, 4.3.3.

- *Why it matters:* The book gives the power to the winner only; the system gives it to both clubs, so a losing club's Owner, GM or AGM can file the combined game today.
- *Options:* (A) Either club's management, within one day of the fixture, with every club filing reported to staff (what the system does TODAY). Book text as in F-6-9 with "the management of either club". (B) The winning club's management only. Needs authForGame to compare the seat against the merged score, which is known only after the merge, so the check would have to run after the merge and refuse the write. (C) Statistics staff only. Remove the management door from authForGame and the Team HQ Game stats tab.
- *Today:* Option A: netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js authForGame accepts the Owner, GM or AGM of either club.

**Q24.** (Q-2-13) **Should the transactions department be able to move a player between a club's active roster and camp on the club's behalf?** Rule 2.1.

- *Why it matters:* The book gives squad moves to club management (and the office only in the freeze); set_roster_squad and swap_roster_squad also admit transactions staff outside the freeze.
- *Options:* (a) Yes (TODAY): add to 2.1.4 'The transactions department may make such a move on a club's behalf to correct an error, and the club is notified of it with the department's reason.' (b) No: withdraw has_department('transactions') from both RPCs.
- *Today:* public.set_roster_squad(uuid,text) and public.swap_roster_squad(uuid,uuid) admit is_team_manager, is_commissioner or has_department('transactions'), with no reason parameter. The club is told only through the ordinary notify_squad_or_block club_notify ('To training camp: X'), with no reason attached. The weekly freeze in guard_squad_move() exempts only is_commissioner() or trusted_writer(), NOT the transactions department. So a department member can make the move outside the Wed 7:30 PM to Fri midnight window but is refused inside it. The site's squad buttons (part_live.js:9382, 9428) are keyed to CG.myClub(), so there is no department UI.

**Q25.** (Q-4-2) **When the Away club vetoes the Home club's only choice and Home named no second choice, should the game go to the Away club's preferred server before the standard server?** Rule 4.2.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 4.2.6 reads as if the standard server is reached only when nothing on either side can be honored, but the resolver skips the Away club's preferred server in this case.
- *Options:* A: consult the Away club's preferred server (if it is not its own veto) before the standard server; requires a change to resolve_game_server. B: go straight to the standard server; requires a sentence in 4.2.6 saying the Away club's preferred server is consulted only when the Home club named nothing. Today the system does B.
- *Today:* B: resolve_game_server sets the result to the (empty) second choice and falls through to the standard server.

**Q26.** (Q-5-5) **Should the lineup tools warn, before a sheet is filed, when a camp player is dressed while an available rostered player under his cap is left out, or should that promise be struck from Rules 5.2.4 and 5.2.full.4?** Rule 5.2.

- *Why it matters:* The book promises a pre-filing warning that no tool gives; with the position lock lifted, 'a rostered player of that group' also needs a meaning.
- *Options:* (a) Build it: the lineup builder and line creator check before submit, using the per-game availability answer for that exact game, and while the lock is lifted judge preference across the whole active roster rather than by group; the book sentence stays, reworded for the lock-off case. (b) Strike the final sentence of Rule 5.2.4 and the matching sentence of 5.2.full.4; preference under Rule 5.2.3 remains a matter the office raises on its own. TODAY neither: no warning exists and only auto-fill ranks camp players last.
- *Today:* public.lineup_camp_warnings is uncalled, not executable by members, reads the sheet after filing and uses night-level availability; no warning reaches a club.

**Q27.** (Q-2-6) **May an Owner nominate a player already under contract, including one on his own roster, as General Manager or Assistant General Manager?** Rule 2.6.

- *Why it matters:* After the draft every rostered player is under contract, so today an Owner can fill a vacant GM or AGM seat only with an unrostered registrant, cannot promote his AGM, and cannot re-nominate a removed manager; the book states no such limit.
- *Options:* (a) No, the nominee must be registered and uncontracted (TODAY); add to 2.6.1: 'A nominee for General Manager or Assistant General Manager must be registered for the season, may hold no player contract, and may hold no league-office role other than Media alone; a player already on a roster may be seated only by the league office.' (b) Yes: relax guard_mgmt_nominee's contract test, state it in 2.6.1, and decide the nominee's salary (see Q28).
- *Today:* Option (a): guard_mgmt_nominee refuses a contracted or unregistered nominee; set_team_manager (commissioner) has no such test.

**Q28.** (Q-2-7) **When a player who earns more than the seat pays is seated, is his prior salary preserved and restored when the seat ends, or does the seat value replace it for good?** Rule 2.6.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.6.3 promises he keeps the larger salary; the system overwrites it with $0 or $2,000,000 and he leaves at the greater of the seat value and $750,000.
- *Options:* (a) Preserve: store the prior salary (for example on the contract) and restore greatest(prior, seat value) on vacating. (b) Replace (TODAY): delete the sentence and write 'A player's salary while seated is the fixed value of his seat, and on leaving the seat he carries the greater of that value and the league minimum.'
- *Today:* Option (b): ensure_manager_rostered overwrites contract and roster salary; _set_team_seat applies greatest(salary, 750000).

**Q29.** (Q-2-16) **When a newly seated manager's position group is already full on the active roster, is the appointment refused, must the club clear a spot, or may he sit in training camp?** Rule 2.6.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.6.3 says a manager holds an active-roster spot in his group; the system parks him in camp and skips the cap check.
- *Options:* (a) Refuse the appointment until room exists. (b) Require the club to clear a spot first. (c) Allow camp (TODAY), and say so in 2.6.3.
- *Today:* Seating runs through the teams trigger trg_team_manager_sync, which calls public.ensure_manager_rostered(uuid,uuid). For an appointee with NO roster spot this season, it INSERTs one under app.mgr_sync, and place_new_roster_spot() parks him in camp ('tc') when his group or the total is full. For an appointee already rostered on ANOTHER club, it UPDATEs team_id. reset_squad_on_team_change() then forces squad='pro', and the deferred constraint trigger check_roster_structure() (no mgr_sync exemption) aborts the whole appointment if the new club's group or total is full, so in that case the appointment is effectively refused. An appointee already on the same club keeps his current squad, camp included.

**Q30.** (Q-6-1) **Should a player who has tended goal in even one league game be rated as a goaltender for all his games, or should the rating follow the position group he has played most, goaltender included?** Rule 6.1.2.

- *Why it matters:* With position locks lifted, a skater who fills in once in goal is re-rated entirely as a goaltender from then on, and his skater games stop counting toward the number members see.
- *Options:* (A) Any goaltending game makes the player a goaltender for rating purposes (what the system does TODAY, public.cghl_group). Book states it as in F-6-4. (B) The group with the most games decides, goaltender included, so an emergency appearance does not re-rate a skater. Needs a one-line change to public.cghl_group and the book text in F-6-4 without the goaltender clause.
- *Today:* Option A: public.cghl_group returns G whenever gp_g > 0.

### Before the movement deadline, the playoffs (Nov 4) or the end of the regular season

**Q31.** (Q-0-1) **Should a league-observed holiday week also be skipped during the playoffs, and should Remembrance Day (Wed Nov 11) and US Thanksgiving (Thu Nov 26) stay on Season 1's holiday list?** Rule 0.8.1 (with 0.7.1, Chapter 8). (also raised as Q-8-4)

- *Why it matters:* It decides when Season 1 ends. With the current list the final is played Dec 2 to 4; without the two holidays it is Nov 18 to 20. 0.8.1 says only 'three game-weeks', which members will read as three consecutive weeks.
- *Options:* (A) Holiday weeks are skipped in the postseason as in the regular season (TODAY'S BEHAVIOR). Keep the list: rounds Nov 4 to 6, Nov 18 to 20, Dec 2 to 4. Book: add to 0.8.1 "A game-week containing a league-observed holiday is skipped in the postseason as in the regular season (Rule 0.7)." (B) Same rule, but untick the two November holidays for Season 1: rounds Nov 4 to 6, 11 to 13, 18 to 20; book gets the same sentence. (C) The postseason is always three consecutive weeks: the generator must ignore holidays for playoff rounds, and 0.8.1 should say "in three (3) consecutive game-weeks, holidays notwithstanding".
- *Today:* Option A: the playoff generator (src/live/part_live.js:12346 to :12350) steps over holiday weeks using the season's list, which includes both November holidays.

**Q32.** (Q-8-1) **In the head-to-head tiebreaker, should clubs be compared on points earned in their games against each other (an overtime loss counts one point) or on wins (an overtime loss counts nothing)?** Rule 8.2.

- *Why it matters:* Two tied clubs can be level on head-to-head points but not on wins (for example, one regulation win against two overtime losses), which changes seeding or a playoff berth.
- *Options:* (a) Points, which is what the system does today: the book should say "head-to-head points earned in games between the tied clubs". (b) Wins: CG.h2hPoints in src/live/part2_engine.js must drop the overtime-loss point, and the book should say "head-to-head wins".
- *Today:* Points: 2 for a win and 1 for an overtime loss among the tied clubs (src/live/part2_engine.js:706-712).

**Q33.** (Q-8-2) **Should the higher seed be the Home club for all seven games of a series, or should home alternate, with the higher seed home on Wednesday and Friday and the lower seed home on Thursday?** Rule 8.3.

- *Why it matters:* The Home club selects Home in the lobby, names the server choices under Rule 4.2.5 and is listed as home on the schedule and box score; the book and the system disagree for two games of every series.
- *Options:* (a) Higher seed home every game: change hostHigher to [true,true,true] in part_live.js and the Control Center caption; the book stands. (b) Alternation, which is what the system does today: amend 8.3.1 and 8.3.full.1 to read "The higher seed is the Home club for the games on Wednesday and Friday, and the lower seed for the games on Thursday."
- *Today:* Alternation: the higher seed is home Wednesday and Friday, the lower seed Thursday (part_live.js:12356).

**Q34.** (Q-8-3) **In the final between the two division champions, which club is the higher seed and holds home designation?** Rule 8.3.

- *Why it matters:* The book gives the higher seed home designation but does not define the higher seed between champions of different divisions; today the result depends on which division is listed first.
- *Options:* (a) The champion with the better regular-season record (points, then the Rule 8.2 tiebreakers): the generator must compare standings, and 8.3.1 should say so. (b) A fixed division order, which is what the system does today: the East champion is always home, and the book should state that. (c) Alternate by season.
- *Today:* The first-listed division's champion (East) is always the higher seed in the final, because the seed list is ordered division by division (part_live.js:12277, 12291-12296).

**Q35.** (Q-6-5) **Is streaming required in playoff games?** Rule 8.3.2, 6.2.2. (also raised as Q-8-5, Q-general-1)

- *Why it matters:* The league's own playoffs announcement says it is required, while the rulebook treats streams only as recommended evidence, so a club could be told it breached a rule that does not exist.
- *Options:* (A) Required: add to Rule 8.3.2 "Each club shall stream every playoff game from at least one player's view" with the consequence the commissioner chooses; nothing in the system would check it, so enforcement is by report. (B) Not required: remove "streaming is required for playoff games" from public.announce_lifecycle()'s playoffs_tonight text. TODAY: the announcement says required; no rule or tool requires it.
- *Today:* public.announce_lifecycle() posts 'playoffs_tonight' to discord_default_webhook with the text 'streaming is required for playoff games'. The live matchup page makes the same claim in two places: CG.ROUTES.matchup in src/live/part5b_public2.js:631 ('Required, at least one stream per club' for stage playoff) and :634 ('Playoff games require at least one stream per club'). This route is not overridden elsewhere. No rule requires streaming ([6.2.2] only says clubs 'should' capture screenshots or stream footage; [3.2.5] mentions stream evidence), and no function, trigger or tool checks it.

**Q36.** (Q-2-14) **May a trade be reversed after the movement deadline, and by whom?** Rule 2.3.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.3.3 sets no time limit and names the transactions department; after the deadline the department is refused by the lock and only a commissioner can reverse.
- *Options:* (a) Only a commissioner after the deadline (TODAY): add to 2.3.3 'A reversal is available to the department until the movement deadline and to a commissioner thereafter.' (b) The department at any time: exempt reverse_trade's status write from the deadline guard.
- *Today:* Option (a): guard_trade_insert applies moves_locked to the status change for non-commissioners.

**Q37.** (Q-9-10-1) **Who votes on the individual season awards: league staff and commissioners only, or also each club's Owner, GM and AGM?** Rule 9.2.1.

- *Why it matters:* The book promises club management a vote it cannot cast; a GM relying on 9.2.1 is wrong, and the public Awards page already says 'staff ballot'.
- *Options:* A (the book): staff, commissioners and club management vote. System: admit holders of a club seat in the award_ballots RLS and add a ballot card to Team HQ. Book: "Individual awards are decided by a vote the league office runs among the commissioners, league staff and club management, other than members of the Media department, who hold no ballot in either capacity (Rule 2.7)." B (the system TODAY): the commissioners and league staff other than Media vote; club management does not. Book: "Individual awards are decided by a vote the league office runs among the commissioners and league staff, other than members of the Media department, who hold no ballot (Rule 2.7). Each voter casts one vote per award and may change it until the award is finalized." No system change.
- *Today:* Option B: RLS admits is_staff() or is_commissioner() and excludes is_media_staff(); the ballot card is on the Staff Desk only.

**Q38.** (Q-9-10-4) **May the commissioner name an award winner other than the ballot leader, or only break a tie?** Rule 9.2.1.

- *Why it matters:* The RPC lets the commissioner award any player regardless of the vote and replace a finalized winner later, while the book says awards are decided by a vote.
- *Options:* A: tie-break only. System: finalize_season_award refuses p_winner unless it is one of the players tied for the most votes; a replacement of a finalized award is allowed only through the stripping power of Rule 9.3. Book: add to 9.2.1 "Where the vote is tied, the commissioner breaks the tie." B (the system TODAY): the commissioner's finalization is the decision and the vote informs it. Book: "Individual awards are decided by the commissioner, informed by a vote ... The commissioner breaks any tie and may correct a finalized award."
- *Today:* Option B in the database (p_winner accepted unchecked; ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE replaces a winner), though the site exposes neither power (see F-9-10-5).

**Q39.** (Q-9-10-8) **Is Rookie of the Year an award in Season 1, when every player is a rookie?** Rule 9.1.1.

- *Why it matters:* The Staff Desk offers the ballot now, and the book neither names the award nor defines a rookie; in Season 1 it duplicates the MVP vote.
- *Options:* A (the system TODAY): Rookie of the Year is on every season's slate. Book: name it in 9.1.1 and define a rookie as "a player in his first CGHL season". System: restrict the ballot pool to rookies (it lists every player today, which matters from Season 2) and include goaltenders in the public front-runner list or state that they are excluded. B: the award starts in Season 2. System: hide the category from the Season 1 ballot. Book: "Rookie of the Year is awarded from the second season."
- *Today:* Option A without a rookie filter on the ballot: rookie_of_year is balloted now with every player eligible.

**Q40.** (Q-3-3) **When the office moves a game into a later calendar week under the exceptional-circumstances clause, does it count toward the week it was scheduled in or the week it is played in?** Rule 3.3.1.

- *Why it matters:* The week decides the six-game appearance cap and the availability sheet. Under the current behavior a player could dress for the moved game plus six games in the new calendar week.
- *Options:* A (TODAY): the original week, because games.week is not changed. Add to Rule 3.3.1: 'A rescheduled game keeps the game-week it was originally scheduled in for every weekly count.' B: the week it is played in. ops_reschedule_game recomputes games.week from the new date, which changes player_week_games for both weeks; add to Rule 3.3.1: 'A game moved into another game-week counts toward the week in which it is played.' Either way, ops_reschedule_game could also refuse a cross-week move unless the reason from F-3-8 is an office ground, to enforce the book's 'will not approve' sentence.
- *Today:* games.week is untouched by a reschedule; public.player_week_games and CG.weekGamesFor count by games.week; no week check on the move.

**Q41.** (Q-5-1) **If a game week's first puck drop is ever earlier or later than 9:00 PM ET, is availability due at 7:30 PM ET that day, or 90 minutes before that first puck drop?** Rule 5.1.

- *Why it matters:* The book states both as if they were the same; they diverge the first time a first game moves. The weekly roster freeze of Rule 2.1.4 is also pinned to 'Wednesday at 7:30 PM, the hour availability is due'.
- *Options:* (a) Fixed hour, 7:30 PM ET on the day of the week's first games (WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES TODAY): the book deletes 'Wednesday in a normal week, 90 minutes before the first puck drop' and reads 'no later than 7:30 PM Eastern Time on the day of the game week's first games, whatever the hour of the first puck drop'; the reminder copy at discord-scheduler.js:880 drops '90 minutes'. (b) Relative, 90 minutes before the week's first puck drop: week_availability_deadline changes to min(scheduled_at) minus 90 minutes; the book keeps the relative words and drops the fixed hour; Rule 2.1.4's freeze is redefined as starting at the availability deadline.
- *Today:* public.week_availability_deadline returns 7:30 PM ET on the date of the week's first non-voided game, regardless of its start time.

**Q42.** (Q-3-6) **Should 'repeated forfeits' be a fixed number that alerts the league office automatically, and if so, how many in a season?** Rule 3.2.6.

- *Why it matters:* Nothing counts a club's forfeits. DAL forfeited 4 of its 9 week-1 games (3 for lateness, 1 under Rule 4.3) and nothing surfaced it; a member would assume the rule triggers a review.
- *Options:* A: set a number N. Rule 3.2.6 reads 'A club that forfeits [N] games in a season is referred to the officiating department for review under Chapter 7', and the staff briefing or the Staff Desk 'Needs attention' card flags the club at N. B (TODAY): leave 'repeated' to the office's judgment and say so: 'The league office reviews a club's forfeits and may refer them under Chapter 7.'
- *Today:* staff_briefing(), weekly_staff_report() and _staff_attention() never mention forfeits, and no DB function or cron job counts forfeits per club or raises an alert at any threshold. However, the Officials' desk (src/live/part9_staffdesks.js:403 to 450) shows officiating staff a 'forfeits & voids' count and a 'Rulings on the record' list that names each forfeiting club (the first 10). The data is in front of the desk; it is simply never tallied per club or flagged.

### Before Season 2, or standing policy

**Q43.** (Q-DRAFT) **How is the Season 2 draft order set: a pure random draw, as Rules 0.5.2, 0.9.2 and 2.8.3 say, or the NHL-style lottery weighted by Season 1's standings and playoff finish that the draft desk preselects?** Rule 0.5.2, 0.9.2, 2.8.3.

- *Why it matters:* It decides who picks first in Season 2. The NHL lottery was built at your request (v2.27, August 25), and the book was later written to say every order is random. Season 1 only came out random because the lottery had no prior season to read.
- *Options:* (A) Random every season: the book stands; the desk defaults to Pure random and refuses the standings styles in a basic season. (B) NHL lottery from Season 2 on: rewrite 0.5.2, 0.9.2 and 2.8.3 to describe the lottery (weakest non-playoff club most tickets, top two picks drawn, playoff clubs after in reverse order of finish, champion last). (C) The commissioner chooses the method each season and announces it before the draft: the book says so and names the options.
- *Today:* The draft desk preselects the NHL lottery (src/live/part_live.js:5185, 5270) and generate_draft_board accepts every style in a basic season; for Season 2 a one-click board would be weighted by Season 1.

**Q44.** (Q-0-3) **Should the draft conclude automatically when the last selection on the clock is made or skipped, or stay open until the commissioner concludes it while any skipped selection remains?** Rule 0.5.2 (with 2.8.6, 2.8.7, 2.8.8).

- *Why it matters:* The book promises that a skipped pick remains the club's until the draft is concluded, which it describes as a commissioner act. Today the draft ends itself, so a club whose final-round pick expires, or whose manager returns after the last pick, loses the pick to a random placement.
- *Options:* (A) Automatic conclusion (TODAY'S BEHAVIOR): public._draft_advance marks the draft complete when no pick is both unused and unskipped. Book: add to 0.5.2 and 2.8.7 "The draft concludes automatically when every selection has been made or skipped and none remains on the clock; a skipped selection not used by then is replaced under Rule 2.8." (B) Commissioner concludes: _draft_advance holds the draft open (for example paused, with the skipped picks listed) while any skipped selection is unused, and only the commissioner's Conclude action ends it; no book change.
- *Today:* Option A: _draft_advance sets status 'complete' and draft_make_pick then refuses with 'The draft is not running.'

**Q45.** (Q-2-23) **Does a manager's contract end with the season like every other contract, or run with his seat into the next season?** Rule 2.5.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.5.3 says every contract expires with its season; start_next_season excludes management contracts from expiry.
- *Options:* (a) Ends with the season: include management contracts in the rollover expiry. (b) Runs with the seat (TODAY): add to 2.5.3 'save a management contract, which continues with the seat at the new season's fixed value'.
- *Today:* public.start_next_season(uuid) expires basic-format contracts only where `not coalesce(is_manager,false)`, so management contracts stay 'active' (their end_season stays 1). The seats on teams are not cleared. Nothing at rollover gives a manager a roster spot for the new season. That happens only when a seat changes (trg_team_manager_sync, which calls ensure_manager_rostered) or when a commissioner runs apply_mgmt_salaries(). Either path rewrites the contract salary to mgmt_salary_of, the seat's current fixed value, and inserts the roster spot.

**Q46.** (Q-2-17) **May approving an owner application found a new club (expanding the league), or only seat an owner in an existing vacant club?** Rule 2.6.

- *Why it matters:* The book is silent; the system creates a new club with its Discord role and room when the awarded franchise is not in the league.
- *Options:* (a) May found a new club (TODAY per the sweep): add 'an award of a franchise not yet in the league admits it as a new club'. (b) Existing vacant clubs only: refuse an award of a franchise not in the league.
- *Today:* Option (a) per set_owner_app_club and _owner_app_autoplace (from the unwritten-rules sweep; not re-verified here).

**Q47.** (Q-2-18) **May a single Applications reviewer decide an owner or staff application alone, bypassing the reviewer vote, and may a decided application be reopened?** Rule 2.6.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.6.1 describes a reviewers' decision; decide_owner_application and decide_staff_application let one reviewer decide outright and do not require a pending application.
- *Options:* (a) Yes (TODAY per the sweep): state it in 2.6.1. (b) No: limit the single-reviewer RPCs to commissioners and refuse a decided application.
- *Today:* The ballot path (application_ballots, then resolve_application_if_ready, then apply_application_decision) needs every reviewer to vote, decides by majority, and only updates status='pending' rows. public.decide_owner_application(uuid,boolean,text) lets ANY has_department('applications') member (commissioners included) decide alone. It sets app.app_decide and has no status check, so a decided, withdrawn or waitlisted owner application can be flipped. public.decide_staff_application(uuid,boolean,text) has the same gate and no status check, but it does NOT set app.app_decide, so for a non-commissioner reviewer guard_app_decision() silently reverts status, decided_by and response to their old values. The staff role grant (on approve) and the denial notice still happen. So a single non-commissioner reviewer's staff decision half-applies, and only a commissioner's single staff decision records cleanly. override_application_decision is commissioner-only and routes through apply_application_decision (pending only).

**Q48.** (Q-2-19) **While a club has no Owner, do its managers keep the access the last Owner set, or act with full access until an Owner is seated?** Rule 2.6.

- *Why it matters:* With the Owner seat vacant, a manager on approval mode cannot queue anything, no nomination can be filed and only a commissioner can remove a manager.
- *Options:* (a) Keep the last table (TODAY): state it in 2.6.2. (b) Full access until an Owner is seated: mgmt_gate must treat approval mode as full when the Owner seat is empty.
- *Today:* The access table does carry over: team_mgmt_policy is never reset when the Owner seat empties (only mgmt_access_for and set_team_mgmt_policy touch it, and no trigger on teams clears it). So pages set to full stay full and withheld pages stay hidden. Pages set to Owner approval are frozen, though. public.mgmt_gate(uuid,text) still returns 'approve', so a direct move fails with MGMT_APPROVAL_REQUIRED. public.mgmt_request_move(text,text,jsonb,text) then refuses to queue it ('This club has no Owner to approve moves'). public.mgmt_decide_move(uuid,boolean,text) lets only the sitting Owner decide, not a commissioner, so moves already pending sit undecided until an Owner is seated.

**Q49.** (Q-2-22) **When a placement would carry the drawn club over the cap, is the player drawn to another club, placed regardless, or reported to the league office?** Rule 2.8.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.8.8 promises every registrant a club; today the cron silently retries on the next sweep, and the on-insert trigger path fails the sign-up itself with a cap error. Latent while every club has cap room.
- *Options:* (a) Draw another club (close to TODAY for the cron): add 'A club without room under the cap for the league minimum is not drawn.' (b) Place regardless, as a league-office act. (c) Report to the office, which places him.
- *Today:* The cap is enforced by trigger trg_guard_roster_cap (public.guard_roster_cap(), BEFORE INSERT on roster_spots), which exempts is_commissioner(). Depth placement in public._assign_reg_random picks a club purely at random (an empty camp first) with no cap filter. In distribute_unproven_rookies and auto_assign_latecomers an over-cap insert is caught per player: a warning is raised and he is skipped. distribute_unproven_rookies also notifies commissioners that players were left unseated. The next scheduled run draws again at random. The first pass, public._place_for_unused_picks, has no handler, so one over-cap insert aborts the whole post-draft placement run and it retries each cycle. public.trg_autoassign_new_reg() has no handler on the late-registrant path, so the player's registration insert fails with 'Over the salary cap'. When a commissioner runs placement with p_force, the cap check is skipped and the player is placed regardless.

**Q50.** (Q-2-24) **What may a staff vote decide, is it binding or advisory, what majority carries it, and are ballots visible to all staff?** Rule 2.7.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.7.2 relies on the idea of a staff ballot, but the system computes no quorum, tally rule or effect, lets the opener delete a vote with its ballots, and shows every ballot to every staffer.
- *Options:* State in Rule 2.7 who may open a vote, who may cast a ballot (Media excluded, as today), the majority that carries it, whether it binds, and whether its opener may delete it after ballots are cast.
- *Today:* Any staffer or commissioner opens a vote; Media have no ballot; no tally rule; opener or commissioner may close or delete (from the unwritten-rules sweep).

**Q51.** (Q-2-5) **Are player salaries and cap hits public or confidential?** Rule 2.5.

- *Why it matters:* Rule 2.5.5 calls contract values confidential (and 2.6.6 makes disclosure a conduct matter), while the site prints every cap hit to signed-out visitors, the transaction log prints signing salaries and Rule 2.3.5 posts the cap hit with a block listing.
- *Options:* (a) Public (TODAY): amend 2.5.5 to 'Every player's salary is public. A club's cap planning, and the terms of its offers and negotiations, are confidential to its management group and the league office.' and 2.5.full.5 alike. (b) Confidential: restrict SELECT on roster_spots.salary and contracts, remove the cap hit from public profiles, the transaction wire and #trade-block, and amend 2.3.5.
- *Today:* Option (a): RLS 'rosters readable by all' and 'contracts_read' are unconditional; public profile shows 'Cap hit'.

**Q52.** (Q-2-11) **Are league-office acts (a commissioner's trade acceptance, roster entry or salary correction, and a management appointment) exempt from the salary cap, and what happens to a club that stands above it?** Rule 2.5.

- *Why it matters:* Rules 2.3.3 and 2.5.1 say the cap is checked at every move without exception; the system skips it for commissioners and for manager seating, and nothing flags or restricts a club left over the cap.
- *Options:* (a) Exempt, with a duty to cure (TODAY in mechanics): add to 2.5.1 'A roster move, trade acceptance or appointment made by the league office is not refused by the automatic cap check; every such act is posted to the transaction log, and a club placed above the cap by it shall return to compliance within the period the league office sets.' (b) Not exempt: run the cap check for commissioner acceptances, roster entries and appointments.
- *Today:* Option (a) without the cure clause: accept_trade and guard_roster_cap skip the check for is_commissioner() and app.mgr_sync; no over-cap consequence exists.

**Q53.** (Q-2-12) **Is salary retention permitted in a basic-format trade?** Rule 2.3.

- *Why it matters:* accept_trade applies a per-player retention (up to 50 percent) sent in the trade row, charging it to the trading club's cap and producing off-lattice cap hits; reverse_trade leaves it in place. The Trade Hub has no control, but any seat can send it through the API.
- *Options:* (a) Not permitted: add to 2.3.1 'No salary is retained in a trade: a traded player's full salary moves with him' and refuse a non-empty retention in the database. (b) Permitted: state the 50 percent ceiling, that the retained share stays on the trading club's cap for the season, and have reverse_trade undo it.
- *Today:* Supported by the database via accept_trade and apply_retention, unused (0 rows), not offered in the UI, silent in the book.

**Q54.** (Q-2-20) **May an office holder who plays, or a Media staffer who holds a club seat, see other clubs' filed lineups, availability and lobby codes before the lock?** Rule 2.7.

- *Why it matters:* Staff RLS shows every club's sheets, availability and codes; several staff and commissioners are rostered players and one staff-role member holds a front-office seat.
- *Options:* (a) Yes, under a conduct duty (TODAY in access): add to 2.7.3 'A member of the league office who plays or holds a club seat has, by virtue of the office, access to other clubs' filed lineups, availability and lobby codes, and shall not use it for his club's advantage; doing so is a conduct violation under Chapter 7.' (b) No: narrow the access for office holders who play or hold a seat.
- *Today:* Option (a) access with no stated duty (from the unwritten-rules sweep).

**Q55.** (Q-1-3) **Should each rule change be announced to members in the Discord, or should Rule 1.3.1 say the site's rulebook is the only authoritative text and members are responsible for the version in force?** Rule 1.3.1.

- *Why it matters:* Members are told that not having seen a posting is no defense, but rule changes are not posted where members can see them: the #rules card is edited silently and the changelog goes to a Commissioner-only channel.
- *Options:* (a) Announce every amendment in a member-visible channel (for example a post in #announcements or #rules when the version changes, naming the rules that changed and the effective date). Book stands. (b) Amend 1.3.1 (WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES TODAY): 'The rulebook is published on the league's website, which is its only authoritative text; the rules channel in the league Discord links to it and shows the version in force. Members are responsible for the version in force, and an amendment takes effect on the date stated in the rulebook's change record.'
- *Today:* Option (b) in practice: one #rules card edited in place when the version changes; changelog posted by hand to #software-updates, which only commissioners can see.

**Q56.** (Q-4-1) **Should the league publish a settings sheet (game configuration, position requirements and the ban lists) as a page on the site, or should the rulebook stop incorporating one and state the configuration itself?** Rule 4.1. (also raised as Q-9-10-6)

- *Why it matters:* Rules 4.1.1, 4.5.2, 4.5.5, 5.2.1 and 10.1.1 incorporate a document that does not exist, so the official game settings and the lineup position requirements cannot be found by a member.
- *Options:* A: publish a settings sheet page (lobby settings, period length, overtime, position requirements, banned abilities and builds), link it from 4.1.1 and the matchup page, and keep the book text. B: strike the incorporation; 4.1 states the lobby settings directly, 4.5 becomes the definitive ban list, and 4.5.2, 4.5.5, 5.2.1 and 10.1.1 drop the phrase. Today: neither; the phrase exists only in the rulebook text.
- *Today:* No page is titled 'settings sheet', but the live matchup page (CG.ROUTES.matchup, src/live/part5b_public2.js:437, card around line 623) shows a 'Lobby settings / League standard' card: mode EASHL 6v6 Club Private, Grudge Match off, Allow Replay Skips on, periods 3 x 4:00, continuous 4:00 OT with no shootout, sides, streaming. tools/guides/src/gamenight.html points players to that card. The card has no position requirements, ban lists or goaltending title thresholds; the ban lists live in Rule 4.5 and the loadout guide.

**Q57.** (Q-4-8) **May a club play a fixture under another CGHL club's own EASHL club, or only under a club outside the league?** Rule 4.6.

- *Why it matters:* 4.6.1 says 'a different EASHL club' without restriction, but the database refuses any EA club a league club owns, so such a game cannot be recorded and would not import.
- *Options:* A: another CGHL club's EASHL club is allowed; guard_game_club_substitution needs a fixture-scoped exception. B: only a club outside the league; 4.6.1 says "an EASHL club outside the league". Today the system enforces B.
- *Today:* B: guard_game_club_substitution raises 'EA club already belongs to ..., so this is a mis-link and not a substitution'.

**Q58.** (Q-4-9) **Should every signed-in member be able to read the whole game incident log, including staff notes and who reported each incident?** Rule 4.3.

- *Why it matters:* The read policy is open to all authenticated users; the book is silent, and the log carries staff notes about specific clubs and players.
- *Options:* A: yes, open to every member; add to 4.3.10 "The game incident log is open to every member of the league." B: no; narrow the read policy to the two clubs in the game and the league office. Today the system does A.
- *Today:* A: pg_policies game_incidents_read SELECT using true.

**Q59.** (Q-6-2) **Should a commissioner's correction of a player's rating persist through later recomputes, or stand only until the next final?** Rule 6.1.2.

- *Why it matters:* The book promises a correction power, but every final in the league recomputes every rating and silently overwrites a hand value, usually within a day.
- *Options:* (A) Persistent: add an override (for example an overall_override column with who, when and why) that refresh_player_overall respects, plus a commissioner control to set and clear it; book keeps "may correct a single rating on the record" and adds that the correction is recorded and stands until withdrawn. (B) Temporary: no change to the system; book says "a commissioner may correct a single rating, and the correction stands until the ratings are next recomputed." (C) No hand corrections: delete the clause; ratings change only through the box scores. TODAY: a correction is possible only by SQL and lasts until the next final (effectively B, with no desk).
- *Today:* No rating-correction desk exists anywhere in src/live (part7_admin, part9_staffdesks). A commissioner can write profiles.overall directly: the RLS policy 'commissioner manages profiles' (ALL, is_commissioner()) allows it, and guard_profile_role lets a commissioner through while silently reverting overall for everyone else. The value is overwritten by refresh_player_overall more often than 'the next final'. It is overwritten when ANY game goes final (trg_games_final_overall recomputes every player), when any game_stats row for that player is inserted, updated or deleted (trg_game_stats_overall), and when a roster_spots row for him is inserted or its position changes (trg_roster_pos_overall). The hand edit also leaves player_position_overalls and the overall_breakdown categories showing the engine's number.

**Q60.** (Q-6-4) **Should the case form gain a "Statistics correction" request, routed to the statistics department and limited to club management, or should Rule 6.3.1 be rewritten to describe a correction as an ordinary complaint to the league office?** Rule 6.3.1, 1.3.2, 7.1.2.

- *Why it matters:* Today a correction request lands with the commissioners and all staff as a general complaint, not with the statistics department that holds the tools, and nothing asks the filer for the game or the entry.
- *Options:* (A) Build it: a new action type (or complaint heading) that asks for the game and the disputed entry, routes through notify_department('statistics'), and is limited to club management per Rule 1.3.2; evidence remains pasted text or links unless attachments are added. Book keeps 6.3.1, with "attaching" changed to "including or linking". (B) Keep the system: book says "A club that believes the official record is wrong files a complaint with the league office, naming the game and the entry disputed and including or linking its evidence." TODAY: B's behavior, with the book describing A.
- *Today:* CG.ACTION_META (src/live/part_live.js:8025) has only complaint, appeal, trade_request and position_change; there is no correction type. CG.COMPLAINT_SUBJECTS (:8031) includes 'Rulebook violation' and 'Something else' but no statistics heading. Any signed-in member may file (RLS ar_insert only checks profile_id = auth.uid()), and the form is a subject, an optional target and free text with no attachments. notify_action_request notifies commissioners, every role='staff' profile (notify_staff_bell) and #casework. In addition, bot/staff-alerts.mjs routes every complaint as a department alert to OFFICIATING, or to community when the subject is Discord behavior. Nothing routes to statistics.
