**Q61.** (Q-7-10) **Should the public (including visitors who are not signed in) see a member's suspensions in full, including the issuing official and the written reason, or only the length and the headings?** Rule 7.2.2.

- *Why it matters:* For a 7.7 ruling the reason is the moderator's quotation of the chat, which may contain the slur itself, and it is published on the profile and readable without signing in. The book never says the record is public.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: every suspension (not warnings) is world-readable with its reason, headings and issuer, and the profile shows reason, length, status and issuer. Book: add to 7.2.2 "Suspensions are a public record: the length, the grounds and the issuing official are shown on the member's profile. Formal warnings are not published." (b) Public sees length, dates and headings; the written words stay with the member and the league office. System replaces the anon policy with a view without the reason column. Book adds the same clause with 'headings' in place of 'grounds'. (c) Nothing public.
- *Today:* Option (a) (RLS 'suspensions are league record' for anon and authenticated where mode <> 'warning'; part5a_public.js:2569).

**Q62.** (Q-7-11) **Should any limit apply to appeals: two per suspension (today), one per sanction, or none, and should the general filing limit ever refuse an appeal?** Rule 7.6.1.

- *Why it matters:* A member reading Rule 7.6 expects one appeal window; the system allows two then refuses, applies the cap only while a suspension is running, and the general limit of five open filings or five in ten minutes can refuse an appeal inside its 48 hours.
- *Options:* (a) TODAY: at most 2 appeals while suspended, counted from the latest active suspension; appeals also count toward the five-open and five-per-ten-minutes limits. Book: add to 7.6.1 "A member may file no more than two (2) appeals against a single suspension." (b) One appeal per sanction, tied to it (F-7-16), exempt from the general limits. Trigger limit becomes 1 and the flood guard skips appeals. (c) No count; appeals exempt from the general limits.
- *Today:* Option (a) (public.cap_suspended_appeals and guard_action_request_flood triggers).

### Dormant: full format only

**Q63.** (Q-2-8) **If a later season sets minimum service above zero, should it gate trades as well as waivers?** Rule 2.4.

- *Why it matters:* Rules 2.4.3 and 10.1.3 say it gates both; v3.40 removed the trade check outright, so only waivers would be refused, and two client strings promise a trade gate.
- *Options:* (a) Both: restore can_move_player() in guard_trade_insert and accept_trade behind the setting. (b) Waivers only (TODAY): amend 2.4.3 and 10.1.3 to 'before his club may waive him', and fix part6_hub.js:2152 and part_live.js:13620.
- *Today:* Option (b): can_move_player is called only by waive_player; the figure is zero now.

**Q64.** (Q-8-6) **In a future full-format season, are the number of qualifiers per division, the series length and the playoff games floor fixed figures of Appendix A, or season settings the commissioner may change?** Rule 8.1.

- *Why it matters:* Appendix A states four qualifiers, best of seven and no games floor as law, but in a full season the system reads each from a Control Center or season setting that can differ.
- *Options:* (a) Settings, which is what the system does today: amend 8.1.full.1, 8.3.full.1 and 8.3.full.3 to say these are season settings published before the draft, as 0.1.3 says for basic. (b) Fixed: make playoff_per_div, playoff_best_of and season_rules ignore site_config and seasons.playoff_min_gp in the full format, and lock the Control Center fields.
- *Today:* Settings: playoff_per_div and playoff_best_of read site_config.playoff_format in full (defaults 4 and 7), and season_rules overlays seasons.playoff_min_gp in any format. Not in force; every season is basic.

## Part 3. Corrections to the book and the system

Ranked by severity. *Target* says whether the book, the system, or both should change; *decision* means one of the questions in Part 2 comes first. The live defects in Part 1 are repeated here in their chapters.

### High (32)

**F-0-1** Rule 0.5, 0.9 (also 2.8.3) (0.5.2, 0.9.2). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* The draft order is drawn at random for each season and bears no relation to the previous season's standings, playoffs or champion; at rollover the order for the next draft is drawn at random without reference to the previous season.
- *The system does:* public.generate_draft_board(integer,integer,text,uuid[]) reads the season format only for the snake flag and the default round count. It accepts every order style (reverse_standings, lottery, nhl_lottery, random, manual, as_drawn) for a basic season. The Control Center draft desk preselects 'nhl_lottery' (src/live/part_live.js:5185, :5270). That style falls back to a random draw only when the previous season has no final regular-season game, which is why Season 1 was random (order_meta fallback 'random'). For Season 2 the default board will weight the order by Season 1's standings and playoff finish unless the commissioner picks 'Pure random' by hand.
- *Evidence:* generate_draft_board: "elsif p_style = 'nhl_lottery' then select s.id into v_prev_id from public.seasons s where s.number = p_season_number - 1 and exists (... final regular games ...); if v_prev_id is null then ... order by random() ... v_fallback := 'random'; else /* prior-season table ... */"; part_live.js:5185 and :5270 default style "nhl_lottery".
- *Correction:* Decision needed first: see the draft-order question (Q-DRAFT).
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-0-7** Rule 0.5 (with 2.8.6, 2.8.7, 2.8.8) (0.5.2). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* A skipped selection remains the club's to use at any time before the draft is concluded, so an absent manager loses his turn but not his pick.
- *The system does:* The draft concludes itself. public._draft_advance sets draft_state.status to 'complete' the moment no pick is both unused and unskipped, and public.draft_make_pick then refuses ('The draft is not running.'). A club whose clock expired can use its skipped pick only while other picks remain on the clock; if the last selection of the draft is the one that expires, that club gets no chance at all. The unused pick is then replaced by a random placement ten minutes later. The book says the commissioner concludes the draft (2.8.8).
- *Evidence:* public._draft_advance(integer): "if v_next is null then update public.draft_state set status='complete' ..."; public.draft_make_pick: "if v_state is null or v_state.status not in ('live','paused') then raise exception 'The draft is not running.'".
- *Correction:* see Q44
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-1-1** Rule 1.1 (1.1.5). Target: system.
- *The book says:* When a rostered or camp player leaves the league Discord, his club is told, he has twenty four hours from the notice to rejoin, and if he does not he is removed and the spot is free.
- *The system does:* The procedure is not running at all. public.sweep_roster_departures(integer) runs its three phases (notice, rejoin, removal) in one transaction. Phase A skips only notices that are still open, so a player who was noticed, rejoined inside the window, and then left again gets a second INSERT on the same (profile_id, season_id) primary key; the 23505 unique violation aborts the whole call on every sync tick. No club is noticed and no window can expire for anyone. A second, latent abort sits behind it: two open notices past their deadline belong to players whose clubs waived them by hand on 2026-09-27 (and whose registrations were then withdrawn), so once Phase A is fixed, Phase C will reach those rows, delete zero roster spots and raise 'Rule 1.1 removal touched no roster spot', stopping the sweep again.
- *Evidence:* app_config rl_discord-sync_result at 2026-09-28 00:42 UTC: ok false, lastError 'POST rpc/sweep_roster_departures -> 409 {code 23505 ... Key (profile_id, season_id)=(f40a2338..., 0f1198ee...)'. Function source: Phase A filter 'n.removed_at is null and n.rejoined_at is null', plain INSERT with no ON CONFLICT; Phase C 'if v_n = 0 then raise exception'. roster_departure_notices: 8 rows, last notice 2026-09-26 02:29 UTC; 1 row rejoined and not removed whose player is out of the guild again; 2 open rows past deadline whose players hold no roster spot and no registration (archived 'left_discord' 2026-09-27 00:08 and 02:28 UTC, waived_at 00:08 and 01:20 UTC). 3 rostered players are out of the guild right now with no open notice. tools/roster-departure.test.cjs has no repeat-departure or already-removed case.
- *Correction:* In public.sweep_roster_departures: (1) Phase A reopens an existing notice instead of inserting a duplicate (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (profile_id, season_id) DO UPDATE SET team_id = excluded.team_id, notified_at = now(), deadline_at = excluded.deadline_at, rejoined_at = null, removed_at = null). (2) Phase C closes, and reports, a notice whose player no longer holds a spot on that club (waived, traded or withdrawn since the notice) instead of raising, and each player is handled under its own savepoint so one bad row cannot stop the sweep for every club. (3) Add both cases to tools/roster-departure.test.cjs. (4) A failed sweep should reach the operations desk, since this one has been failing with nothing but an ok:false row in app_config. The book text stands once the sweep runs.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-1-14** Rule 1.3 (1.3.1). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* Rule changes are delivered through Discord, every rostered player is responsible for information posted there, and not having seen an official posting is no defense.
- *The system does:* Nothing announces a rule change automatically. discord-scheduler.js:216 buildRulesLink and rulesUpkeep keep a single card in #rules and PATCH it in place when the rulebook hash changes, which pings nobody. Change notes go to #software-updates (discord_updates_webhook), which only the Commissioner role can see. The server does have a commissioner-written #announcements channel (discord-sync.js:852 ensureAnnouncements, with the 'CGHL Announcements' webhook), so the office can post a rule change there by hand. The only automated writer to that webhook is public.announce_lifecycle (season lifecycle), and no code posts rulebook changes.

**F-2-5** Rule 2.3 (2.3.1, 2.3.3). Target: system.

No trigger validates status transitions (guard_trade_insert checks only the deadline and picks; trg_mgmt_gate_trades only applies the Owner's access). A seat can write 'accepted' without any player moving (notify_trade_status then tells both rooms the trade is complete), write 'reversed', revive a declined offer, or relabel a completed trade so reverse_trade refuses it.

Book text may then add to 2.3.1: 'An offer may be declined only by the club that received it and withdrawn only by the club that made it; a completed trade is undone only by reversal under Rule 2.3.3.'

**F-3-1** Rule 3.2 (3.2.4). Target: system.

Neither compares auth.uid() with the two clubs' roster spots or management seats. In Season 1, 4 of 4 officiating staff, 3 of 3 commissioners, 1 of 2 statistics staff and the 1 operations staffer hold roster spots, so a staffer can lift a forfeit in his own club's game and nothing refuses it. Another member of league staff must rule on it (Rule 3.2.4).' The book text already states the rule and needs no change. **F-4-2** Rule 4.3 (4.3.9). Target: system.

Since v3.18 a first sitting short of regulation is held with status 'scheduled', but the abandonment sweep reads only games already 'final', and forfeit_abandoned_game refuses anything not final. A held game instead goes to the statistics desk three hours after puck drop, with a notice offering a Rule 4.3 merge or a Rule 3.2 forfeit. The automatic ruling has never fired, and the officiating desk is not told.
- *Evidence:* netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js:784 (complete = elapsed >= REGULATION_S or went_ot, otherwise held); ingest-stats.js:1591 closeOutAbandoned queries status=eq.final; public.review_held_games(integer) sends notify_department('statistics', ...) naming 'Rule 4.3' or 'somebody forfeited (Rule 3.2)'; select count(*) from admin_audit where action='abandoned_forfeit' returns 0; public.forfeit_abandoned_game(uuid,uuid,text) is not executable by authenticated.
- *Correction:* No decision needed: Rule 4.3.9 and the commissioner's 2026-09-23 ruling (sql/2026-09-23-abandoned-forfeit.sql) require the automatic ruling. Make the abandonment sweep read held games, not only finals, and give forfeit_abandoned_game a path for a held game; tell the officiating desk each time.

**F-4-3** Rule 4.3 (4.3.9). Target: system.
- *The book says:* An abandoned game is a forfeit: the club ahead is credited the win on the score as it stood, the club behind is recorded as forfeiting, and BOTH clubs keep every statistic.
- *The system does:* public.forfeit_game(uuid,uuid,text,boolean) on the default path deletes all game_stats and writes 1-0. With p_keep_result it raises 'Import and merge the played sessions first' unless status='final'. public.declare_forfeit (officiating) refuses any game with box scores. So for a held game no single door produces the Rule 4.3.9 outcome, and the review_held_games notice offers 'Rule 3.2' forfeit as one of two options. But a site route does exist, and it is not the correction card or SQL. The correction card (src/live/part_live.js:2701) lists only final games, so it cannot pick a held game. The 'League lag-out merge' card (/api/ingest-stats leagueMerge, ingest-stats.js:1370-1500) accepts a single sitting, rebuilds the box score and writes status 'final' with the played score. The Forfeit card's 'Three disconnections (Rule 4.3), keep the played result' option then applies forfeit_team_id and keeps every statistic. The forfeit card's caption tells staff to merge first, but the option is labeled for three disconnections, not for an abandoned game.
- *Evidence:* public.forfeit_game(uuid,uuid,text,boolean): 'delete from public.game_stats where game_id = p_game' on the default path; keep-result path raises 'Import and merge the played sessions first' unless status = 'final'; public.review_held_games(integer) notice text; public.stats_game_set_result(uuid,integer,integer,boolean,boolean).
- *Correction:* Give statistics and officiating staff one door for an abandoned game (for example forfeit_game with a third mode, or a staff-callable wrapper of forfeit_abandoned_game) that finalizes a held game on its played score, keeps every game_stats row, sets forfeit_team_id to the club behind, refuses a level score, and announces the result in the game scores channel. Rewrite the review_held_games notice to name that door and Rule 4.3.9 instead of a Rule 3.2 forfeit. This is needed whichever answer Q-4-4 (settled, Part 4) receives, because a level abandoned game is always ruled by hand.
- *Checked:* second check corrected the description

**F-4-4** Rule 4.3 (4.3.7). Target: both.
- *The book says:* The game is published as a forfeit loss, and that ruling stands even where the forfeiting club finished with more goals.
- *The system does:* The site honors the ruling, but the database's playoff series counter and clinch calculator count wins by goals only. A Rule 4.3 forfeit in which the forfeiting club scored more goals would be counted as a win for the forfeiting club when deciding whether a playoff series is over and whether a club has clinched. The series trigger also runs only when a game first becomes final, so a kept-result forfeit ruled afterwards never re-runs it.
- *Evidence:* public.trg_conclude_series(): counts home_score > away_score, returns early when old.status = 'final'; public.check_playoff_clinches(uuid) reads home_score and never mentions forfeit_team_id; src/live/part2_engine.js:522-532 (client standings honor forfeit_team_id).
- *Correction:* System: make trg_conclude_series and check_playoff_clinches decide each game by forfeit_team_id first and goals second, and re-evaluate a series when forfeit_team_id changes on a final playoff game. Book, add to Rule 4.3.7: "The forfeit ruling decides the result for every purpose, including the standings, clinching, and the count of wins in a playoff series."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

The check is made by group, because the game reports a defenseman without saying which side he played." 4.6.3, replace "his position group under Rule 2.1" with "his position group under Rule 2.1, where the position lock is in force".

**F-4-6** Rule 4.2 (4.2.3). Target: system.

**F-7-3** Rule 7.2 (7.2.2). Target: both.
- *The book says:* A suspended player may not dress ... while the suspension runs (the suspension has an end).

A games or date suspension stops being enforced by the database guards when it ends (is_suspended turns false), but the row stays 'active' forever; only season-length rows are ever closed automatically. Every screen therefore keeps calling the member suspended (public profile, Users and roles, Officials and Community desks), the lineup builder keeps refusing to dress him, and the forum guard, which reads the row rather than is_suspended, keeps refusing his posts, until an official presses Lift, which is the tool the book uses for vacating a sanction.

- *Correction:* System: mark a games or date suspension served when is_suspended would clear it (a 'served' status set by the existing cron or computed on read), mirror the games and date modes in the client's served test at part_live.js:866, and make _forum_guard use is_suspended. Book: add to Rule 7.2.2: "A suspension ends of itself when its last game has been served or its end date has passed, and a suspension through a named season ends when that season is complete. The member is then restored to every privilege it withheld without any further act of the league office, and the suspension remains on his record as served."

**F-7-4** Rule 7.2 (7.2.2). Target: system.

- *The system does:* The database does not refuse a suspended player on the per-game lineup that is actually used. set_game_lineup refuses a suspended caller (the manager submitting it), never the six players named. game_lineups has no triggers. The player-level trigger trg_block_suspended_lineup sits on the legacy lineups table, which holds 0 rows and has no live writer. The only guard is the browser lineup builder.
- *Evidence:* public.set_game_lineup(uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid,boolean): the only suspension check is 'if is_suspended(v_uid) then raise ... Suspended members cannot set lineups.' pg_trigger: 0 triggers on game_lineups; trg_block_suspended_lineup is on public.lineups (0 rows). part6_hub.js:1018 is the client-only refusal.
- *Correction:* System: in set_game_lineup, set_team_line, set_team_line_night and set_team_game_line, refuse any named player for whom is_suspended is true; retire the dead trigger on the legacy lineups table. The book is correct and needs no change.
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-7-5** Rule 7.2 (7.2.2). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* Suspensions carry across season boundaries where unserved games remain.
- *The system does:* A games suspension counts down only on the finals of the club the player was on when it was issued, in the season it was issued. When that season ends with games unserved, the count freezes and the suspension never ends until an official lifts it by hand. A player traded or waived mid-suspension keeps serving on his former club's games.
- *Evidence:* public.is_suspended(uuid) games branch: team_final_count(s.team_id, s.season_id) minus start_game_count, with team_id and season_id frozen at issue; no function ever updates them.
- *Correction:* see Q5
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-7-6** Rule 7.2 (7.2.1). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* Sanctions may be combined where a single course of conduct violates multiple rules.
- *The system does:* Sanctions never stack. Issuing a new suspension silently lifts every active suspension the member already has, unserved games included, with no notice; suspend_player also closes his active warnings. The newest ruling replaces the rest.
- *Evidence:* public.suspend_player: 'update public.suspensions set status=''lifted'' ... where profile_id=p_profile and status=''active''' before the insert (warnings included). public.discipline_from_case: the same update restricted to mode <> 'warning'.
- *Correction:* see Q6
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-7-7** Rule 7.6 (7.6.2). Target: both.
- *The book says:* Appeals are decided by a panel that excludes any official who imposed the original sanction.
- *The system does:* There is no panel and no exclusion of the issuer. An appeal is an ordinary case that any one non-media official may resolve or deny; the conflict checks exclude only the filer and the named subject. An appeal carries no link to the suspension it appeals, so the database cannot tell who issued it.
- *Evidence:* RLS action_requests ar_update excludes only profile_id and target_profile_id; public.discipline_from_case the same; suspensions.created_by is never consulted; no suspension_id on action_requests; application_ballots exist for applications only.
- *Correction:* System (needed under any answer to Q9): an appeal records the sanction it appeals, and the case refuses resolution, denial or discipline by that sanction's issuing official. Book: the composition of the deciding body is a decision, see Q9.
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-7-8** Rule 7.2 (7.2.2). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* A suspended player may not ... serve as a club's point of contact for game operations; the book lists no other consequence.
- *The system does:* The point-of-contact bar is not enforced: public.night_board(date), public.resolve_game_server(uuid) and public.rotate_game_code(uuid,text) never reference suspensions. Worse, the dress bar is not enforced on the live lineup either. public.set_game_lineup(...) checks only the CALLER (is_suspended(v_uid)), never the six players it dresses. The trigger that refuses a suspended player, block_suspended_lineup, sits on the legacy public.lineups table (0 rows), not on public.game_lineups (83 rows, no triggers). set_team_line, set_team_game_line and set_team_line_night also check only the caller. The item's actor list is right in substance, but its mechanism is partly wrong. The caller guard is is_suspended(auth.uid()) in set_game_lineup, clear_game_lineup, set_team_line*, set_jersey_number, waive_player, sign_free_agent, offer_free_agent, offer_extension and mgmt_request_move. block_suspended_actor fires only on trades and contract_offers (INSERT/UPDATE). RLS blocks the rest: game_vetoes_write (server veto), lineups_write, and roster_spots 'gm updates own team roster' (so trade-block listing, which is a direct roster_spots update). The triggers on contracts (block_suspended_contract) and roster_spots (block_suspended_roster) check the PLAYER, not the actor. They add consequences the book never states: a suspended player cannot be put on a roster (a commissioner is exempt) or sign a contract, sign_free_agent, offer_free_agent and respond_offer refuse him, auto-placement skips him (auto_assign_latecomers and similar), and cap_suspended_appeals limits him to 2 appeals. public._forum_guard() blocks posting for any 'active' non-warning suspension row. Nothing ever flips a served games or date suspension out of 'active' (status is only active or lifted), so the forum bar outlasts the suspension until someone lifts it by hand. Still allowed: set_roster_squad and swap_roster_squad (call-up and send-down, SECURITY DEFINER, no check), draft_make_pick, and the club lag-out desk in netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js (no suspension check). A suspended official keeps suspend_player, discipline_from_case, lift_suspension and assign_case; none of them checks the actor's own suspension.
- *Evidence:* is_suspended(auth.uid()) guards in set_game_lineup, set_team_line*, waive_player, sign_free_agent, offer_free_agent, offer_extension, respond_offer, mgmt_request_move; block_suspended_actor triggers on trades, contract_offers, contracts, roster_spots; _forum_guard. No suspension reference in set_roster_squad, swap_roster_squad, draft_make_pick, night_board, resolve_game_server, rotate_game_code; ingest-stats.js authorize() (per the sweep). No actor check in suspend_player, discipline_from_case, lift_suspension, forum_moderate.
- *Correction:* see Q4
- *Checked:* second check corrected the description

**F-8-1** Rule 8.3 (8.3.4, 8.3.full.3). Target: system.
- *The book says:* A player is eligible to be dressed in the playoffs only if he ... is not under suspension for the game in question.
- *The system does:* The database does not refuse a suspended player on a per-game lineup. public.set_game_lineup checks is_suspended() only for the member filing the sheet (v_uid), not for any of the six players named. public.lineup_slot_ok checks roster and position only. The suspension trigger trg_block_suspended_lineup is attached to the legacy table public.lineups; public.game_lineups (the table in use since v2.44) has no triggers at all. Only the lineup builder in the browser greys out suspended players, and nothing after a game compares suspensions with the box score. The same gap applies to the regular season under Rules 5.2.1 and 7.2.2.
- *Evidence:* public.set_game_lineup: 'if is_suspended(v_uid) then raise exception ''Suspended members cannot set lineups.''' (filer only); pg_trigger on public.game_lineups: none; pg_trigger on public.lineups: guard_lineup_positions_trg, guard_tc_weekly_cap_trg, trg_block_suspended_lineup; client only: src/live/part6_hub.js:877-878, 953-954, 1018
- *Correction:* In public.set_game_lineup, refuse the sheet when is_suspended() or is_banned() is true for any named player (or attach a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger equivalent to block_suspended_lineup to public.game_lineups). Add an after-final check, on the model of check_playoff_violations, that flags any suspended player found in game_stats. The book text is correct as written.
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-9-10-9** Rule 10.1 (10.1.1). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* "dress" means to appear in a league lobby as a participant in a game.
- *The system does:* The rest of the book and the system use 'dress' for naming a player on the filed lineup. Rules 5.3.3 and 5.1.3 speak of a club that 'dresses' a player and may 'take him off', and of a dressed player who does not appear. The site's week ledger labels a player 'Dressed, did not play' and 'Not dressed'. The forfeiture triggers in 5.2.1 and 8.3.3 turn on 'dressing' an ineligible player: under the 10.1.1 definition an over-cap box-score appearance is a dressing and forfeits automatically, but the system enforces eligibility on the filed sheet (set_game_lineup refuses an ineligible player) and sends a box-score deviation to the officials' desk under Rule 5.3 'as the case requires', not to automatic forfeiture. The weekly cap counts box-score appearances for played games and filed sheets for games not yet played.
- *Evidence:* chapter-10.md [10.1.1] vs chapter-5.md [5.3.3], [5.1.3], [5.2.1], chapter-8.md [8.3.3]. src/live/part_live.js:218 to 224 (sat: 'Dressed, did not play', counts:false; filed: counts:true; out: 'Not dressed'). memory/clearing-a-box-score-finding.md ('Rule 5.2's mandatory forfeiture attaches to dressing an ineligible player, which a legal sheet plus an in-game switch does not do'); memory/unrostered-player-flag.md (box-score findings routed to Officiating).
- *Correction:* see Q3
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-general-1** Rule None (system defect). It bears on Rule 5.3.2, which promises that the notice of a late lineup change is the club's current sheet. ([5.3.2] (indirectly); no paragraph states the rule). Target: system.
- *The book says:* The book says a lineup change after the lock is reported automatically to the opposing club's front office, on the site and in its club room, so that the sheet the opponent sees is the current one.
- *The system does:* The three helpers that write those notices, public._post_lineup_notice(p_game uuid, p_team uuid, p_old game_lineups, p_new uuid[], p_diff integer, p_uid uuid, p_locked boolean), public._notify_lineup_pulled(p_game uuid, p_team uuid, p_was uuid[], p_why text) and public._notify_lineup_moves(p_game uuid, p_team uuid, p_old game_lineups), are SECURITY DEFINER, executable by anon and authenticated, and check no caller. Anyone, signed in or not, can post a forged 'Lineup set/changed' notice into any club's room under any actor, or send any member a 'You are no longer dressed' notification with arbitrary text. The re-check found the hole is wider: public.club_notify(p_team, p_kind, p_title, p_body, p_actor, p_view, p_param, p_ping_role, p_room), which _post_lineup_notice calls, is itself SECURITY DEFINER and executable by anon and authenticated with no caller check. It notifies a club's three front-office seats and inserts into club_notices (the club's Discord room feed) with any title, body, actor and role mention the caller supplies. tools/sql/grant-audit.sql does not catch any of the four: the three helpers write only through PERFORM of another function, so its 'insert into|update|delete from' test is false, and club_notify matches the guard pattern only because it uses auth.uid() as a default actor, not as a check.
- *Evidence:* has_function_privilege('anon'|'authenticated', ..., 'execute') is true for all three helpers and for public.club_notify; prosecdef true; none reference auth.uid as a guard. Only callers: set_game_lineup (_post_lineup_notice, _notify_lineup_moves) and clear_game_lineup (_notify_lineup_pulled). club_notify body: 'insert into public.club_notices(... actor_profile_id ...) values (p_team, p_kind, p_title, p_body, coalesce(p_actor, auth.uid()), ...)'. public.create_notification is correctly not executable by anon or authenticated. Grant-audit regex run against the three helpers: writes=false, guard_match=false.
- *Correction:* Revoke EXECUTE on _post_lineup_notice, _notify_lineup_pulled, _notify_lineup_moves and club_notify from public, anon and authenticated (their callers are SECURITY DEFINER and keep access). Before revoking club_notify, confirm no client code calls it through the API. Then extend tools/sql/grant-audit.sql so that (a) a SECURITY DEFINER function that PERFORMs a known writer counts as a writer, and (b) auth.uid() appearing only inside coalesce() does not count as a guard. No book change is needed.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

- *The system does:* The Control Center season editor's Auto-space, the tool that spaces the calendar, writes the draft time as 7:00 PM ET in both the basic and the full branch. Season 1 (2026-09-20 01:00Z) and Season 2 (2027-02-14 02:00Z) hold 9:00 PM ET only because the time was corrected by hand after spacing. A commissioner who runs Auto-space and saves gets a 7:00 PM draft, contradicting 0.5.1, 2.8.1 and the draft room's own '9:00 PM ET' copy. The draft time is not a format rule (it is not in format_rules), so the 7:00 PM in 0.5.full.1 has no format reason; the draft moved to 9:00 PM for every draft in v2.62.
- *Evidence:* src/live/part_live.js:11857 put("ssDraft", CG.etISO(draftDay,"19:00")) (basic branch) and :11902 the same in the full branch; part_live.js:4288, :4293 '9:00 PM ET'; seasons.draft_at for S1 and S2; memory/season-format-switch-v248.
- *Correction:* System: make both Auto-space branches write 21:00 ET for the draft. Book: in 0.5.full.1 change "held live on the site at 7:00 PM Eastern" to "held live on the site at 9:00 PM Eastern".

**F-1-10** Rule 1.1 (1.1.3). Target: system.

The book is already correct.

**F-1-16** Rule 1.1 (1.1.5 (with 2.4.1)). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* If he does not rejoin, he is removed and the spot is free for the club to fill.
- *The system does:* public.sweep_roster_departures(integer), called every 2-minute discord-sync tick, runs in any season whose status is not 'complete' with no moves_locked check. Its Phase C sets app.office_removal = '1', which public.guard_roster_waive() lets through ahead of its moves_locked refusal. public.office_withdraw_registration sets the same flag. After the deadline the club is told 'the spot is free', but sign_free_agent and offer_free_agent refuse under moves_locked, and auto_assign_latecomers returns 0 when moves_locked. A club can still fill a vacated ACTIVE-roster spot by calling up a camp player (set_roster_squad and guard_squad_move check only the weekly Wed-Fri freeze, not moves_locked). It cannot add anyone from outside, so a vacated camp spot, or a roster with no camp players, stays short.

**F-1-2** Rule 1.1 (1.1.1). Target: system.
- *The book says:* The console name is how a club adds him and gets him into a party, and it is shown to his club's management and to league staff rather than publicly.
- *The system does:* No page shows a player's console name to his club's Owner, GM or AGM. It is displayed only in the Control Center user editor (league office). At the same time the column is not private: signed-out visitors cannot read it, but every signed-in member can read every member's console name through the profiles table, whose row policy is open to all and whose column grant to authenticated includes platform_gamertag. public.player_contact(uuid), which returns console details, exists but is executable by no member and is called by nothing.
- *Evidence:* has_column_privilege('authenticated','public.profiles','platform_gamertag','select') = true; anon = false; policy 'profiles readable by all' (SELECT, qual true). src/live/part_live.js:1467 loads platform_gamertag for management but :1451 maps the pool to {profileId, tag, pos, ovr, eaId} and drops it; the only render is the Control Center edit field at part_live.js:6933. grep of src/live finds no Team HQ, roster, lineup or draft render.
- *Correction:* Show each player's console and console name to his own club's Owner, General Manager and Assistant General Manager on the Team HQ roster, served by public.player_contact(uuid) granted to authenticated with a check that the caller holds a seat on the player's club or is league office; then revoke SELECT on profiles.platform_gamertag from authenticated (moving the member's own profile read, part_live.js:3702, to the same RPC or a self-only path). The book text then holds as written.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-1-3** Rule 1.1 (1.1.2 (and 1.1.full.2)). Target: system.
- *The book says:* Each player may hold exactly one CGHL identity.
- *The system does:* Uniqueness is enforced on profiles.gamertag and on ea_player_id (partial unique index), not on ea_id, and require_registration_details() does no duplicate check. One lower(btrim(ea_id)) group currently holds two profiles. One is registered, rostered and carries an ea_player_id. The other has no registration, no roster spot and no persona id. The linking claim is wrong: the matcher (netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js:440-456, also used by bot/ea-poll.mjs) resolves on EA's persona id first, so the rostered player's lines link. He has 2 linked game_stats lines, and none with his persona are unlinked. The second profile is not a player and has no lines. The real gap is that nothing stops two profiles from claiming one EA ID. Whether this pair is one person with two accounts or two people cannot be told from the data.
- *Evidence:* pg_indexes on profiles: profiles_gamertag_key UNIQUE(gamertag), profiles_ea_player_id_key UNIQUE(ea_player_id) partial; no unique index on ea_id. Grouping lower(btrim(ea_id)) finds 1 group with more than one profile.
- *Correction:* Resolve the one pair of profiles that share an EA ID now, then add a unique index on lower(btrim(ea_id)) where the EA ID is not blank, so a second profile cannot claim an EA ID already on file; the profile save and the registration details prompt should state the refusal plainly. The book text stands.
- *Checked:* second check corrected the description

- *The system does:* In a full-format season, public.auto_assign_latecomers(boolean) calls _assign_reg_random(reg, 'latecomer_random', false, 'fill'). 'fill' tries in order: 'exact' (a club under roster_max with room in the player's group), 'any' (another group's open seat), then 'overflow' (uncapped, onto the club carrying the fewest active players). So a club below a full roster is tried first, but the overflow seats him regardless. The origin is exempt from check_roster_structure() and pro_roster_count(), which exclude latecomer_random. The row is inserted with salary 0, but the BEFORE INSERT trigger trg_apply_contract (public.apply_contract_on_roster()) raises it to greatest(0, 750000) = $750K and writes a one-season contract. The item's 'at salary 0' claim is therefore wrong: he ends up at the league minimum. The batch notice calls it 'Placed for the pre-season on the same terms as any loan (Rule 0.4)', and public.preseason_release_loans(uuid,text) deletes only origin 'preseason_random', so latecomer_random placements are never released.
- *Evidence:* public.auto_assign_latecomers(boolean) non-basic branch; public.check_roster_structure() (excludes latecomer_random); public._assign_reg_random 'fill' mode
- *Correction:* Before any full season, place full-format late registrants within the shape at the league minimum and report the unseatable (as distribute_unproven_rookies does), with a regular-season notice; or rewrite 2.2.full.3 to the behavior.
- *Checked:* second check corrected the description

**F-3-5** Rule 3.2 (3.2.4). Target: decision.

In bot/incidents.mjs, the forfeit ruling's detail ('This one is not waivable.', line 24) goes only to the late club's room; the ready club's room gets 'This one is ruled in your favor.' The other line, 'The ten-minute forfeit is not waivable.' (line 183), goes to the ready club's room, and only on a 5 to 9 minute penalty ruling. So both rooms can be told the forfeit is not waivable, but on different rulings, not both on the forfeit ruling. tools/incident-rulings.test.mjs:19 pins the wording. Meanwhile undo_forfeit and unforfeit_game let staff lift any forfeit, and [3.2.4] lets an uninvolved staffer waive it.

**F-3-6** Rule 3.2 (3.2.5). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* If neither club fields a lineup and no forfeit is claimed, the game has no result: the commissioner voids it.

The item says the dialog is commissioner-routed, and it is not: the Officials' desk (src/live/part9_staffdesks.js:434 'Forfeit / void' button, wired at :985 to CG.declareForfeitPrompt) shows officiating staff the same dialog, including the 'Neither club played → void' option. So both the UI and the RPC deliberately let an officiating staffer void a game, while [3.2.5] says the commissioner voids it.
- *Evidence:* public.declare_forfeit(uuid,uuid,boolean): gate is_commissioner() or has_department('officiating'); void branch when p_void or p_forfeit_team is null; public.undo_forfeit(uuid) same gate; src/live/part_live.js:11700 to 11720.
- *Correction:* see Q22
- *Checked:* second check corrected the description

**F-3-7** Rule 3.2 (3.2.2). Target: both.
- *The book says:* A club may concede a forfeit before puck drop if it knows it cannot field six.
- *The system does:* No club can concede on the site: every forfeit function is staff-gated, so a concession is a message to the league office that a staffer enters. Two desks can enter a Rule 3.2 forfeit and they behave differently: public.declare_forfeit (commissioner or officiating) refuses a game that already has box scores and writes an audit row and a staff casework post; public.forfeit_game (statistics staff) deletes any box score the game has, sets 1-0, and writes no audit row at all. The book names neither desk.
- *Evidence:* public.declare_forfeit(uuid,uuid,boolean) (log_admin_action 'forfeit_declared', notify_staff_ch 'casework'); public.forfeit_game(uuid,uuid,text,boolean) (delete from game_stats; no log_admin_action); callers part_live.js:2865, 11716; DB: DAL's three Fri Sep 25 games were entered as forfeits by staff.
- *Correction:* Book, replace the last sentence of Rule 3.2.2 with: "A club that knows it cannot field six may concede the game before puck drop by notice from its management to the league office. Every forfeit under this rule, conceded or not, is entered by the officiating or the statistics department of the league office." System: make public.forfeit_game write log_admin_action('forfeit_declared', ...) with the ruling and the calling official, and post to the casework channel, as declare_forfeit does, so every forfeit leaves the same record whichever desk enters it.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-3-8** Rule 3.3 (3.3.1). Target: both.
- *The book says:* A game may be rescheduled only by written agreement of both clubs' management, filed with and approved by the league office before the originally scheduled puck drop.
- *The system does:* public.ops_reschedule_game checks only that the game is not final and that the new time is no more than a day in the past. It never compares now() with the original puck drop, so an agreed reschedule can be entered after the game should have been played. It also takes no reason, so an agreed move, an outage move under Rule 3.3.2 and a replay ordered under Rule 3.2.5 all look the same in the audit log, and nothing asks whether a one-sided failure is being turned into a reschedule. A hard 'before puck drop' guard alone would wrongly block outage moves and replays, which by nature happen after puck drop.
- *Evidence:* public.ops_reschedule_game(uuid,timestamptz) (checks status = 'final' and p_at < now() minus one day only; logs {from, to}); src/live/part9_staffdesks.js:577 to 604 (date and time only).
- *Correction:* System: give ops_reschedule_game a required reason ('agreed', 'league_outage', 'replay'); refuse 'agreed' once now() is past the game's original scheduled_at; record the reason in log_admin_action; add the reason picker to the Operations desk prompt. Book, add to Rule 3.3.2: "The league office may also move a game after its original puck drop where it has ordered a replay under Rule 3.2.5, or where a league-wide outage prevented the game from being played. The league office records the ground for every move."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

It is also one post in the shared management announcements channel naming the clubs that owe, not a message to each club, and nothing is posted when every club has filed.

Book, replace the last sentence of 4.2.7 with: "On the day of each game night, in the regular season and the playoffs, the league office posts one reminder in the management announcements channel ninety (90) minutes before the night's first puck drop, one hour before the freeze, naming each club that has not yet filed. No reminder is posted where every club has filed."

**F-4-11** Rule 4.3 (4.3.7). Target: both.
- *The book says:* The game is published as a forfeit loss, marked FFL on the result.

In Discord the game scores channel already announced an ordinary 'Final' when the merge landed, and the statistics-staff ruling (forfeit_game with keep_result) writes only forfeit_team_id, which fires no announcement and writes no audit row. **F-4-12** Rule 4.3 (4.3.9). Target: both.
- *The book says:* Where the clubs did in fact intend to resume, the officials reverse the ruling and the sittings are merged as usual.
- *The system does:* Both reversal doors return the game to 'scheduled' and null the score. On a forfeit that kept a played score this erases the played result rather than lifting only the forfeit. If the continuation then arrives the merge restores a result; if it never does, the game sits on the schedule with a box score and no score. The reversal is open to officiating (undo_forfeit) and statistics (unforfeit_game), not only the officials.
- *Evidence:* public.undo_forfeit(uuid) and public.unforfeit_game(uuid): 'status = scheduled, home_score = null, away_score = null, went_ot = false, forfeit_team_id = null'; memory/forfeit-news-channel (known, not fixed).
- *Correction:* System: when the forfeit kept a played score (game_stats present and the score is not the 1-0 forfeit score), lifting it clears only forfeit_team_id and leaves the game final on its played score. Book, replace the last clause of 4.3.9 with: "Where the clubs did in fact intend to resume, the officiating or the statistics department lifts the ruling and the sittings are merged as usual. Lifting a forfeit that kept a played score restores that score as an ordinary result; lifting a forfeit under Rule 3.2 returns the game to the schedule. Every reversal is announced where the forfeit was announced."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-4-7** Rule 4.1 (4.1.2). Target: both.
- *The book says:* CGHL does not use a shootout in any game, and no CGHL game can end in a tie.

It is held for the league office, which orders the remaining play or rules the game, and it does not enter the standings until then."

**F-4-8** Rule 4.1 (4.1.3). Target: both.
- *The book says:* A game decided in overtime is a win for the club that scores and an overtime loss for the club that does not.
- *The system does:* Overtime is read from EA's result code by equality with 5 or 6, but the field is a bit set, so a merged lag-out game can lose its overtime flag and be recorded as a regulation loss (one point short) until statistics staff correct it. A correction that changes only the overtime flag moves a point in the standings and is announced nowhere. The book does not tell a club how to report a missed overtime.
- *Evidence:* netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js:135 (wentOt = result === 5 or 6); memory/overtime-flag-and-result-correction (PIT 4-3 UTA filed as regulation, corrected by hand); public.notify_discord_game_final() posts a 'Corrected' line only when the score changes.
- *Correction:* System: decode overtime from the result bits rather than by equality, and have notify_discord_game_final announce a change of went_ot on a final game. Book, add to Rule 4.1.3: "Overtime is read from the game's record. Where a game decided in overtime is recorded as a regulation result, either club may tell the league office, which corrects the record. Every correction that changes a club's points is announced in the game scores channel."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

Once it exists the book text of 4.2.2 is accurate as written; optionally add: "Where a code is disclosed after release, the league office issues a fresh code to both clubs directly."

- *The system does:* Only the over-cap appearance is reported (one notice per game to the officiating desk and the staff casework channel); nothing forfeits automatically. An appearance off the filed sheet is checked nowhere; a club that filed no sheet at all is only logged to admin_audit with no alert. Rule 5.3.3's final sentence then stands unopposed.

- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-5-5** Rule 5.1 (5.1.4 (with 8.3.2)). Target: system.
- *The book says:* Every game week has the 24-hour availability reminder, the day-of lineup request and afternoon reminder, and the notice when the window closes.
- *The system does:* All of these run for regular-season weeks only. In playoff weeks availability can still be submitted and is still stamped late against a playoff deadline, but no reminder, no lineup call and no closing nudge is sent.
- *Evidence:* public.availability_nudge_tick(): loops only coalesce(g.stage,'regular') = 'regular' and builds 'w'||week keys. netlify/functions/discord-scheduler.js:834 (availabilityReminder) and :671 filter (g.stage || 'regular') === 'regular'. public.week_availability_deadline(uuid,text) maps 'po' keys to the playoff stage, and notify_availability_submitted labels 'Playoff round N'.
- *Correction:* Extend availability_nudge_tick, availabilityReminder and lineupReminder to playoff week keys ('po' plus the round) before 4 November 2026. If the office chooses not to, add to Rule 8.3.2: "The availability reminders and notices of Rule 5.1 and the lineup reminders of Rule 5.1.4 are not sent in the playoffs; clubs and players remain bound by the deadlines themselves."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-5-6** Rule 5.1 (5.1.1 (with 2.7.3)). Target: system.
- *The book says:* The system stamps the time of every answer and marks a post-deadline answer late; the record cannot be back-dated.
- *The system does:* A rostered player who holds the staff or commissioner role is never marked late, whenever he answers. Because availability can be written only by the player himself, the exemption applies to nothing but office holders' own answers.
- *Evidence:* public.stamp_availability(): late is set only when 'not (public.trusted_writer() or public.is_commissioner() or public.is_staff())'. RLS on availability: insert and update only where profile_id = auth.uid(). Rule 2.7.3: staff and commissioners may play as rostered players.
- *Correction:* Remove the is_commissioner() and is_staff() terms from the late test in stamp_availability (keep trusted_writer() for service writes). No book change is needed; Rule 5.1.1 and Rule 2.7.3 already treat an office holder as any other player.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-5-7** Rule 5.1 (5.1.4). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* A rostered player who does not submit availability at all in consecutive weeks is reviewed by the league office under Chapter 7.
- *The system does:* Nothing counts consecutive misses or raises a player to any desk. The only trace is the availability_nudges ledger. The nudge DM itself tells the player that two weeks in a row puts him under league-office review.
- *Evidence:* public.availability_nudge_tick() message: 'Two weeks in a row without availability puts you under league-office review (Rule 5.1).' No function, view, desk card or notice reads availability_nudges across weeks. memory/inactivity-ledger-plan.md: escalation (step 3) NOT built; three policy decisions open. The first possible two-week miss is Week 2 (closes Wed Sep 30).
- *Correction:* see Q15
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-5-8** Rule 5.2 (5.2.1 (also 5.2.full.1, 5.2.4)). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* An eligible lineup satisfies the position requirements of the settings sheet, including a rostered goaltender.
- *The system does:* The database accepts a sheet with any slot empty, including goal, and while the position lock is lifted any player on the roster may be filed in goal. The roster-removal trigger blanks a departed player's slot and leaves the sheet on file. Rule 5.2.4 (lock not in force: any player at any position) already conflicts with 'a rostered goaltender'.
- *Evidence:* public.set_game_lineup: array_remove(array[...], null), no minimum count; public.lineup_slot_ok returns on a null player and returns before any group test when position_locks_on() is false. Season 1: 83 filed sheets, 14 incomplete, 3 with no goaltender. public.clear_lineups_on_roster_remove() sets the slot to null. The builder alone refuses ('Fill every slot first', part6_hub.js:1143).
- *Correction:* see Q16
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-5-9** Rule 5.2 (5.2.4, 5.2.full.4). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* Where a club dresses a camp player while a rostered player of that group is under his weekly cap and available for the night, the tools say so before the lineup is filed.
- *The system does:* No tool warns. public.lineup_camp_warnings exists but nothing calls it, members cannot execute it, it reads the sheet already on file (so it could only warn after filing), it tests the night-level availability state rather than the per-game answer, and it compares position groups although the lock is lifted. Only auto-fill ranks camp players last.

**F-6-1** Rule 6.1 (6.1.1). Target: book.
- *The book says:* The official record controls "once the league office has verified the game under this chapter."
- *The system does:* There is no verification step. A game becomes final the moment the importer files it: the standings move, #game-scores announces it and every rating is recomputed, with no human action. The only human touch points are corrections after the fact under Rule 6.3.
- *Evidence:* Enum game_status holds only scheduled, final, forfeit; netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js:800-806 PATCHes status 'final' straight from the EA payload; triggers on_game_final_notify_discord and trg_games_final_overall fire AFTER UPDATE on games; no column, status or audit action records a verification.
- *Correction:* Replace the last sentence of 6.1.1 with: "In any conflict between the official record and a club's own records, EA's in-game screens, or third-party trackers, the official record controls from the moment a game is filed under this chapter, subject to correction under Rule 6.3." (A true verification gate would require a pending state ahead of the standings and #game-scores that does not exist and would contradict the automatic import of Rule 6.2.1.)
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-6-10** Rule 6.2 (6.2.1). Target: decision.

- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-6-13** Rule 6.3 (6.3.1). Target: decision.

- *The system does:* CG.ACTION_META (part_live.js:8025-8030) offers complaint, appeal, trade_request and position_change. CG.COMPLAINT_SUBJECTS (:8031) has no statistics or record heading. CG.fileActionRequest (:8318-8370) has no game or entry field; only the details placeholder asks 'in which game or channel. Link any evidence.' Files cannot be uploaded; a reply in the case thread can carry one http(s) link as an attachment (:8456-8460). The ar_insert RLS lets any signed-in member file for himself. notify_action_request() notifies commissioners, the staff bell and #casework, and bot/staff-alerts.mjs routes complaints to the officiating room (community for Discord behavior). Nothing routes a complaint to statistics.
- *Evidence:* src/live/part_live.js:8024-8029 (CG.ACTION_META) and :8030 (CG.COMPLAINT_SUBJECTS, no statistics heading); action_requests columns subject, details, target; public.notify_action_request() never calls notify_department('statistics').

**F-6-14** Rule 6.3 (6.3.1). Target: both.
- *The book says:* (partial coverage) The league office rules on correction requests.

The guards (no level score, overtime only on a one-goal margin) exist only in the Stats Manager page. A score change posts a "Corrected" line to #game-scores.
- *Evidence:* public.stats_game_set_result(uuid,int,int,bool,bool) (source read: is_stats_staff() check, then a bare update of games); Stats Manager card at src/live/part_live.js:2688-2790; public.notify_discord_game_final() contains the 'Corrected' post.
- *Correction:* System: move the two guards into stats_game_set_result (refuse a level final score; refuse went_ot unless the margin is one goal) and log each call to admin_audit with the prior score. Book, add to 6.3.1: "Corrections to a filed result are entered by the statistics department, are logged, and are announced in the game scores channel with the result they replace. No corrected result may be level, and an overtime result is decided by a single goal."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-6-16** Rule 8.3 (8.3.2 (with 6.2.2)). Target: decision.

**F-6-5** Rule 6.1 (6.1.2). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* "Ratings are not hand-edited: the commissioner may correct a single rating on the record", in the same paragraph as "recomputed for every player after every final."
- *The system does:* A desk DOES exist: Control Center > Users & roles > Edit (CG.userEditModal, src/live/part_live.js:6936 field 'Overall rating', :6953 payload) writes profiles.overall. It is commissioner-only in effect: RLS 'commissioner manages profiles', and public.guard_profile_role() reverts overall for anyone not trusted_writer()/is_commissioner(). It accepts 40 to 99, below the 50 floor. The value does not stick. public.refresh_player_overall() unconditionally overwrites profiles.overall, and it runs for every rated player on every new final (public.trg_game_final_overall), for that player on any game_stats insert, update or delete (trigger trg_game_stats_overall), and on any roster_spots insert or position change (trg_roster_pos_overall), including a position change saved from the same modal. There is no override column or corrected marker. The hand value also never reaches player_position_overalls, so the breakdown card disagrees with it.
- *Evidence:* public.guard_profile_role() returns early for trusted_writer() or is_commissioner(), otherwise reverts overall; public.trg_game_final_overall() calls refresh_player_overall for every player with a final regular or playoff game on every final; public.refresh_player_overall() does update profiles set overall=v_ov unconditionally.
- *Correction:* see Q59
- *Checked:* second check corrected the description

**F-6-7** Rule 6.2 (6.2.1, 6.2.2). Target: book.
- *The book says:* 6.2.1: "For a game to import, it must be played in the correct private lobby (the game's 6-digit code)." 6.2.2: play outside the assigned lobby "breaks the automatic import."
- *The system does:* The importer sees only private club matches, so "private" is enforced, but EA's payload carries no lobby code and the importer never reads one. Any private match between the two scheduled EA clubs that ends inside the window files as the league game, whatever lobby it was played in. Wrong-lobby play does not break the import; only an unrecorded outside club or play outside the window does.
- *Evidence:* bot/ea-poll.mjs:112 requests matchType=club_private; netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js matches on club ids and the window only, with no code check; memory/ea-payload-fields.md (no lobby field); public.set_game_code() issues 6-digit codes.
- *Correction:* 6.2.1, second sentence: "For a game to import, it must be a private match between the two clubs' linked EA clubs, or under a club substitution recorded for that fixture under Rule 4.6, and it must finish inside that fixture's game window, from ten (10) minutes before its scheduled puck drop to three (3) hours after. Clubs play in the private lobby issued for the game under Rule 4.2; the import does not read the lobby code." 6.2.2, last sentence: "Repeated play outside the assigned lobby is subject to discipline under Chapter 7, whether or not the game imports."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-6-8** Rule 6.2 (6.2.1). Target: book.
- *The book says:* (omission) The book describes the merge of a lagged-out game but not the hold that precedes it.
- *The system does:* A sitting that ends short of regulation (3600 game-clock seconds) without overtime is held: its box score is stored and its EA match id stamped, but no score or final status is written, so the standings do not move and nothing is announced. If the rest has not arrived three hours after the scheduled puck drop, the statistics department (every statistics staffer and commissioner) is flagged once, and the game stays unplayed until a person rules.
- *Evidence:* netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js:783-798 (complete = elapsed >= REGULATION_S or went_ot; otherwise PATCH without status or score); public.review_held_games(3) called from netlify/functions/discord-sync.js:2459, calling notify_department('statistics', 'flag', 'A held game never finished', ...) and logging admin_audit held_game_stalled; changelog v3.18.
- *Correction:* Add to 6.2.1 after the sentence on replays: "A sitting that ends before the end of regulation, and did not reach overtime, is not a result: its box score is kept, but no score is published and the standings do not move until the sittings of the game are combined under Rule 4.3. Where the rest of a held game has not been played by three (3) hours after its scheduled puck drop, the game is referred to the statistics department and has no result until that department rules on it." Add the same hold sentence to Rule 4.3.3.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-6-9** Rule 6.2 (6.2.1 (with 4.3.3, 4.3.7)). Target: book.
- *The book says:* 6.2.1: a game that finished outside its window "is filed by the league's statistics staff from the club game stats desk, not by the robot"; lag-out games are listed among the games "the automatic record cannot cover", while the same paragraph and Rules 4.3.3 and 4.3.7 say the import merges them.
- *The system does:* Merging is automatic in the ordinary case (the importer matches the continuation to the held sitting). Where it cannot, three doors file the game: the club game stats desk (Team HQ, #/hub/gamestats) for a club's Owner, GM or AGM, limited to sittings on the fixture's ET night or one day either side, and able to file a single late sitting as well as merge several; the Stats Manager (#/hub/statsmgr) for statistics staff and commissioners, with no day limit; and the commissioner's Re-ingest of an archived payload (see F-6-12). Staff do not work from the club desk. Voided games cannot be merged by anyone, and a game carrying a forfeit ruling only by statistics staff. Every club merge is reported to staff.
- *Evidence:* netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js authForGame :1074-1106 (commissioner or statistics staff via 'staff'; Owner/GM/AGM of either club via 'management', subject to mgmt_access_for 'gamestats'); leagueMerge :1410-1449 (mgmtDays limit for management; norms.length > 1 ? mergeSegments : norms[0]; voided refused); tellStaff :1111; src/live/part_live.js:2932 (Stats Manager) and :12857 (club desk) both call leagueMerge.
- *Correction:* Replace the last two sentences of 6.2.1 with: "The sittings of a disconnected game are combined automatically where the import can match the continuation to the sitting it continues. Where it cannot, and where a game genuinely finished outside its window (a night that ran long past three hours), the game is filed through the league's stats tools: by club management from the club game stats desk, for sittings played within one day of the fixture (see Q23 for which club), or by statistics staff from the stats manager at any time. Every filing made by a club is reported to the league office, a voided game is never filed, and a game carrying a forfeit ruling is filed only by statistics staff. A forfeit has no EA match to import and is entered by the league office under Rule 3.2, or filed automatically for an abandoned game under Rule 4.3.9." Make Rule 4.3.3 say the same in one sentence.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-7-10** Rule 7.2 (7.2.2). Target: both.
- *The book says:* A member is told of a sanction at the moment it is imposed, on the site and, where he has linked his Discord account, by direct message; the notice states the length and the grounds.
- *The system does:* Expulsion, the heaviest sanction, sends no notice of any kind: ban_player creates no notification and no direct message, the reason is optional, and the member learns by meeting the lockout screen and being removed from the Discord within minutes, so the 48-hour appeal clock of Rule 7.6 starts from a notice that never arrives.
- *Evidence:* public.ban_player(uuid,text): seats vacated, roster spots deleted, contracts ended, profiles.banned set; no create_notification, no discord_dms insert; reason stored as nullif(btrim(p_reason),''). discord-sync.js:1736 applies the guild ban on the next run.
- *Correction:* System: ban_player requires grounds and sends the member a site notice and a direct message stating them; the direct message must be queued before discord-sync removes him from the guild, since the bot cannot message a user who shares no server with it. Book: add to Rule 7.2.1: "Every sanction, including a formal warning and an expulsion, states its grounds in writing when it is imposed."
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-7-11** Rule 7.2 (7.2.2). Target: system.
- *The book says:* A member is told of a sanction ... by direct message; the notice states the length and the grounds.
- *The system does:* A formal warning (a sanction under Rule 7.2.1) reaches the member only as a site notification, never by direct message, and may be issued from a case with no reason at all, in which case the notice says 'No reason was recorded.'

**F-7-12** Rule 7.1 (7.1.2). Target: both.

The filing dialog tells the member that commissioners are notified.

Commissioners are notified instantly.'
- *Correction:* Book: replace the first clause of Rule 7.1.2 with "A complaint is announced the moment it is filed to the department that must act on it, and every official is notified that it was filed:" and keep the rest of the sentence. System: the dialog at part_live.js:8349 names the department that receives the filing (officiating, or community for Discord behavior).

**F-7-13** Rule 7.1 (7.1.2). Target: book.

Trade requests are routed to a club's front office and no staff member sees them, which is correct under Rule 2.3 but contradicts 'every filing'.

A trade request is filed with the club's front office under Rule 2.3 and is not a filing with the league office."

**F-7-14** Rule 7.1 (7.1.2). Target: system.
- *The book says:* No official may rule on a case they filed or a case about themselves, so a complaint naming an official is decided by someone else.
- *The system does:* For staff the guard works only when the complaint's subject is linked to a profile. The subject picker is optional, so a complaint that names an official only in its text carries no conflict guard, and that official may rule on it. (The commissioner exemption is a separate decision, see Q-7-3 (settled, Part 4).)

**F-7-15** Rule 7.5, 7.7 (7.5.1, 7.7.2). Target: system.
- *The book says:* A player's full disciplinary history is before the league office in every matter; the community department weighs the member's history under this rule.

From a case, the History button works only for a rostered player and fails with 'No rostered player matches' for a free agent, an unrostered member or a member between clubs, who are exactly who a Discord complaint may name. The Community desk Moderation flow shows no history at all. Prior cases are matched on the gamertag string, so a renamed member loses them.

**F-7-16** Rule 7.6 (7.6.1). Target: system.
- *The book says:* The appeal must identify the ruling appealed and state the grounds; it is filed within 48 hours of the sanction being posted, and sanctions not appealed within the window are final.
- *The system does:* None of this is enforced. The appeal form asks only for a kind of ruling from a list and free text; it is not tied to a specific sanction, does not ask which of the three grounds is claimed, and has no time check, so a late appeal files normally and the reviewing official must notice it is out of time. Evidence can be attached only to a later message in the case thread, not to the filing.

The book is correct and needs no change.

**F-7-17** Rule 7.6 (7.6.2). Target: system.
- *The book says:* The panel may uphold, reduce, or vacate a sanction; games already served are credited if the appeal succeeds.

The re-issued suspension counts from the club's current finals (no credit for games served), carries no headings, and appears on the record as a new sanction with a new date.

- *The system does:* Nothing detects it. The importer already inspects every box-score line for roster and position problems and flags them to the Officials desk, but a suspended player appearing in a filed game raises nothing and the game is recorded normally.
- *Evidence:* public.review_game_records, night_board, resolve_game_server, rotate_game_code: no reference to suspensions (consolidator query). ea-poll.mjs and ingest-stats.js do not consult is_suspended (per the sweep).
- *Correction:* System: review_game_records flags any box-score line whose linked profile was suspended at the game's start, to the Officials desk, as it flags an unrostered player. The consequence for the club and the player is a decision, see Q8.
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-8-14** Rule 8.3 (8.3.1, 8.3.full.1). Target: decision.

- *The system does:* The round generator makes the lower seed the Home club for the two Thursday games of every series, and the Control Center caption attributes that alternation to Rule 8.3.

**F-8-15** Rule 8.3 (8.3.1 (also 0.8.1)). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* A division's first seed ... begins with the division championship in the following game-week; 0.8.1: three rounds in three game-weeks.
- *The system does:* The playoff generator steps over any week that contains a skipped holiday. Season 1 skips Remembrance Day (Wed Nov 11) and US Thanksgiving (Thu Nov 26), so the rounds fall Nov 4 to 6, Nov 18 to 20 and Dec 2 to 4, not in consecutive weeks.

**F-8-16** Rule 8.3 (8.3.2). Target: decision.

A 'published playoff format' document does not exist.

**F-8-2** Rule 8.2 (8.2.2, 8.1.3). Target: system.
- *The book says:* A forfeit is a regulation result and never awards the overtime-loss point ... the ruling decides the win and the loss, not the goals.
- *The system does:* The site table (part2_engine.js) and the Discord table honor the ruling, but two database functions decide wins by goals alone and never read forfeit_team_id. public.trg_conclude_series counts series wins by home_score > away_score, so a playoff game forfeited under Rule 4.3 while the forfeiting club led on goals would count as that club's win, and could close a series or crown the champion the wrong way while the site bracket shows the opposite. public.check_playoff_clinches scores points the same way and awards the overtime point whenever went_ot is true, so a kept-result forfeit could produce a false clinch announcement. No such row exists today (all forfeits so far were also losses on the scoreboard), so the defect is latent.
- *Evidence:* public.trg_conclude_series(): 'count(*) filter (where (g.home_team_id=new.home_team_id and g.home_score>g.away_score) ...'; public.check_playoff_clinches(uuid): 'when g.status=''final'' and g.went_ot then 1'; public.forfeit_game(uuid,uuid,text,boolean) with p_keep_result=true keeps the played score and went_ot; client src/live/part2_engine.js:526-531
- *Correction:* In both functions, count a win as (forfeit_team_id is not null and forfeit_team_id <> club) or (forfeit_team_id is null and the club's score is higher); award the overtime point only when forfeit_team_id is null; exclude voided games. The book text is correct as written.
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-8-3** Rule 8.2 (8.2.1). Target: system.
- *The book says:* Ties in the standings are broken in the following order: head-to-head record between the tied clubs; regulation wins; goal differential; goals scored; and, if still tied, a one-game play-in scheduled by the league office.
- *The system does:* The weekly Discord standings post (#standings) sorts clubs level on points by total wins, then goal differential. It skips head-to-head, uses all wins rather than regulation wins, and omits goals scored, so two clubs level on points can appear in a different order in Discord than on the site.
- *Evidence:* netlify/functions/discord-scheduler.js:481 '.sort((a, b) => b.pts - a.pts || b.w - a.w || (b.gf - b.ga) - (a.gf - a.ga))'
- *Correction:* Make the weekly post use the site's tiebreaker order (head-to-head points, regulation wins, goal differential, goals scored), ideally from one shared implementation so the two tables cannot drift again.
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-8-4** Rule 8.2 (8.2.1). Target: system.
- *The book says:* If still tied, a one-game play-in scheduled by the league office.

CG.standings leaves the pair in whatever order the rows arrived (a code comment only mentions the play-in), and the projected bracket, the clinch news and the round 1 generator would all seed on that arbitrary order without warning. There is no tool for scheduling a single extra regular-season game.
- *Evidence:* src/live/part2_engine.js:790 comment 'still level after all four: Rule 8.2 sends it to a one-game play-in the office schedules'; CG.generatePlayoffRound (part_live.js:12262-12270) has no residual-tie check
- *Correction:* Have CG.standings mark a residual tie, show it on the standings page, and have generatePlayoffRound(1) refuse while a residual tie affects qualification or seeding, telling the commissioner a play-in game is needed. Provide a way to add the single play-in game. The book text is correct as written.
- *Checked:* held under adversarial verification

**F-8-5** Rule 8.3 (8.3.3). Target: system.

The basic cap in force is four for every player. A manager planning from the page would under-use skaters and plan a goaltender for games the lineup tool will refuse.

**F-8-6** Rule 8.3 (8.3.3). Target: system.
- *The book says:* No player may be dressed in more than four (4) games of a single series.

The database gate and check_playoff_violations use the correct series cap of 4; this is display only, but it is the one place members would see a violation.

**F-8-7** Rule 8.3 (8.3.3, 8.3.full.3). Target: book.
- *The book says:* Dressing a player beyond the cap is treated as dressing an ineligible player under Rule 5.2 and forfeits the game.

Then public.check_playoff_violations notifies the commissioners and logs the event after the game is final; it does not enter a forfeit. The forfeit is a human ruling through public.forfeit_game or public.declare_forfeit. The book reads as if the forfeit were automatic.

- *Correction:* Replace the last sentence of 8.3.3 with: "Dressing a player beyond the cap is treated as dressing an ineligible player under Rule 5.2. The league office reviews any box score that shows a player beyond the cap and declares the forfeit of the game in which the cap was exceeded."

**F-9-10-1** Rule 9.2 (9.2.1). Target: decision.
- *The book says:* Individual awards are decided by a vote the league office runs among league staff and club management.

Club management has no ballot: the award_ballots RLS refuses any insert or update by a member, and the ballot card exists only on the Staff Desk. The commissioners, whom the paragraph does not mention, do vote. The Media exception, which Rule 2.7.2 states and the system enforces, is missing from 9.2.1, so the two paragraphs disagree on their face (a Media staffer may also hold a club seat, making him both staff and club management). The public Awards page already tells members that season hardware is decided by staff ballot.

**F-9-10-10** Rule 10.1 (10.1.3). Target: both.
- *The book says:* "Season settings" means the figures the commissioner determines and publishes for each season before its draft: the size and composition of the active roster, any training-camp limit, the number of draft rounds, the playoff eligibility floor, and whether the position lock is in force.
- *The system does:* Only three of the five are per-season figures in the system: draft rounds (seasons.draft_rounds), the playoff floor (seasons.playoff_min_gp) and the position lock (seasons.position_locks). The roster's size and composition (lines, flex, position quota) and the camp limit are format figures in format_rules; guard_season_format refuses a season row whose roster_max differs from the format's, and there is no per-season camp limit column. A commissioner cannot set a different roster or camp limit for one season without a database change. Separately, Rule 0.1.3 says THREE figures are season settings (roster, draft rounds, playoff floor), while 10.1.3 lists five and adds minimum service.
- *Evidence:* public.season_rules(uuid) merges only draft_rounds and playoff_min_gp from the seasons row over format_rules(format); public.guard_season_format() raises when new.roster_max is distinct from format_rules(format).roster_max; season_rules(current season) returns lines 2, flex 3, quota F9 D7 G5, camp_max 999 from the format. chapter-0.md [0.1.3]. memory/rulebook-register-and-season-settings.md (commissioner's v2.50 ruling that roster size and composition are season settings).
- *Correction:* System: carry the roster composition (lines, flex, position quota), the camp limit and minimum service on the seasons row, merge them in season_rules, check them in guard_season_format against the season row rather than the format, and expose them in Control Center, Seasons, Season settings beside draft rounds and the playoff floor. Book: replace the first sentence of [0.1.3] with "The following figures are season settings rather than fixed provisions of this rulebook: the size and composition of the active roster and any training-camp limit (Rule 2.1), the number of rounds in the entry draft (Rule 2.8), the number of regular-season games a player needs to be dressed in the playoffs (Rule 8.3), whether the position lock is in force (Rule 2.1), and minimum service (Rule 2.4)."
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-9-10-11** Rule 10.1 (10.1.3). Target: system.
- *The book says:* "Minimum service" means the number of regular-season appearances a player must have before his club may trade or waive him (Rule 2.4); it is a season setting and the figure in force is zero.
- *The system does:* The figure in force is zero and restricts nothing, as stated. Two latent differences: (1) min_service_gp is read from format_rules, not the seasons row, so it cannot be set for one season (covered by the system half of F-9-10-10); (2) if a figure were set, only waivers would be refused, because v3.40 deleted the trade-side calls to can_move_player in accept_trade and guard_trade_insert. Rules 10.1.3 and 2.4.3 both say a figure would govern trades and waivers alike.
- *Evidence:* public.min_service_gp(uuid) = coalesce((season_rules(p_season)->>'min_service_gp')::int, 0); public.season_rules does not merge min_service_gp from the seasons row; public.can_move_player(uuid,uuid) comment: v3.40 made it 'the WAIVE check only' and 'the two call sites in accept_trade and guard_trade_insert were deleted'; its message ends 'before he can be waived'. chapter-2.md [2.4.3] ('where one is set the league's roster tools refuse a move of a player short of it').
- *Correction:* Restore the minimum-service check in accept_trade and guard_trade_insert, gated on min_service_gp > 0 so it changes nothing while the figure is zero, and reword can_move_player's message to cover a trade or a waiver. With F-9-10-10, the figure then becomes settable per season. The book already says what this produces.
- *Checked:* confirmed by a second independent check

**F-9-10-2** Rule 9.2 (9.2.1). Target: decision.

This matters in practice: all 3 commissioners and 7 of the 8 staff hold a roster spot this season. The phrase 'first-place vote' also implies a ranked ballot, but each voter casts a single pick per award (UNIQUE (season_id, category, voter_id), one profile_id), so there is no second or third place.

**F-9-10-6** Rule 9.3 (9.3.1). Target: system.

The ballot pool lists every player, the award_ballots RLS and constraints do not reference suspensions, finalize_season_award performs no eligibility check, and compute_potw selects from game_stats with no suspension test. public.is_suspended is called by 25 functions (lineups, contracts, roster moves) but by none of the award functions.

Apply the same test in compute_potw so a player serving such a suspension is passed over for Player of the Week. The book text needs no change.
