Twenty-four hours before the window closes each club room names and pings every player with no answer (camp players included, since the list comes from availability_missing), or says the club is fully answered; the front offices of all clubs get one league-wide summary of sheets still to file and answers outstanding. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 5.1.4 after the reminder sentence: "At the same time the league office posts a summary to the front offices of all clubs stating how many lineups each club has yet to file for the week and how many answers are outstanding; the summary is informational and sets no deadline." *Mechanism:* discord-scheduler.js availabilityReminder(): club-room post mentioning each missing player by Discord mention (gamertag if unlinked) from availability_missing, plus one post in #management-announcements pinging the CGHL Management role with sheets filed per club and the outstanding count. *Needs an answer first:* Q14.
- **Rule 5.1.4** (low). The closing notice goes out within 15 minutes of the deadline by site notification and Discord DM, with one line per club; a player added to a roster more than 36 hours after the deadline is never nudged for that week. *Proposed:* No clause needed beyond Rule 5.1.4 as written; optionally add "within fifteen (15) minutes of the window closing" after "When the window closes". *Mechanism:* pg_cron 'availability-nudge' every 15 minutes calling public.availability_nudge_tick(); acts only within 36 hours after the deadline; ledger availability_nudges.
- **Rule 5.2.2** (medium). A player's weekly count follows the player, not the club: games he played or was filed for with a former club earlier in the same game week count against his cap at his new club. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 5.2.2: "A player's weekly count follows the player and not the club. A player who changes clubs during a game week carries into his new club every game counted against him that week, whether played or filed, with his former club." *Mechanism:* public.player_week_games(uuid,text,integer,uuid,uuid) counts by profile across every game of the week with no filter on club.
- **Rule 5.3** (low). The line creator keeps saved lines per club as private planning aids; they never dress anyone, and dressing always passes through set_game_lineup and its checks. *Proposed:* Optional, in Rule 5.3: "Saved lines are private planning aids of club management and are not a filed lineup." *Mechanism:* public.set_team_line, set_team_line_night, set_team_game_line_plan (line creator); RLS limits reads to the club's management and commissioners (reported by the sweep, not re-checked).
- **Rule 5.3.1** (low). A club may take a filed sheet down until its game locks; after the lock only a commissioner can withdraw it. The club room and each player on the sheet are told. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 5.3.1: "After the lock a filed lineup may be withdrawn only by a commissioner. A withdrawal is announced in the club's room and to each player who was on the sheet." *Mechanism:* public.clear_game_lineup(uuid,uuid): refused at or after scheduled_at minus 30 minutes unless is_commissioner(); club_notify to the club room, _notify_lineup_pulled to the six players, admin_audit 'lineup_withdrawn'.
- **Rule 5.3.1** (low). A club that has filed nothing by the lock may still file until ten minutes after puck drop, and a club that never files is only recorded, with no notice and no sanction. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 5.3.1: "A club that has filed nothing by the lock may still file until ten (10) minutes after the scheduled puck drop. A club that files no lineup at all is recorded by the league office but is not penalized for that alone; its appearance at puck drop is governed by Rule 3.2." *Mechanism:* public.set_game_lineup accepts a first filing after the lock until scheduled_at plus 10 minutes; public.review_game_records logs 'lineup_not_filed' to admin_audit only.
- **Rule 5.3.1 (and 2.3.1)** (medium). When a player leaves a club by waiver, removal, departure sweep or trade, he is blanked from every lineup that club has filed for a game still scheduled, including sheets already past the lock. The club (site and room) is told how many sheets now have an empty slot and to re-file, and the player is notified once per game. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 5.3.1: "A player who leaves a club's active roster or training camp by any means, a trade included, is removed at once from every lineup that club has filed for a game not yet played, even one already locked. The club and the player are notified, and the club may fill the empty place within the time this rule allows." *Mechanism:* triggers clear_lineups_on_roster_delete_trg (AFTER DELETE) and clear_lineups_on_roster_move_trg (AFTER UPDATE OF team_id) on roster_spots, calling public.clear_lineups_on_roster_remove().
- **Rule 5.3.2** (low). A post-lock change that only moves the same six players between positions is not reported to the opponent or in #management-moves; only the club's own room is told. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 5.3.2: "A change that only moves the same six players between positions is not reported to the opponent." *Mechanism:* public.set_game_lineup computes v_diff as players changed, not slots, and calls _post_lock_notices only when v_diff > 0.
- **Rule 5.3.3** (low). A club's own room and its three seats are told each time its sheet is filed, changed (including a pure position reshuffle) or withdrawn, with the six in rink order; a save that changes nothing posts nothing. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 5.3.3: "Each lineup a club files, and each later change to it or withdrawal of it, is posted in the club's own room and notified to its front office, so that the whole club sees the sheet as it stands." *Mechanism:* public._post_lineup_notice (called from set_game_lineup) and club_notify in clear_game_lineup.
- **Rule 6.1 (new 6.1.3)** (medium). Every result is announced in #game-scores when it becomes final: Final (with OT where it applies), Final (forfeit) naming the forfeiting club, Voided, a Corrected line whenever a final score later changes (with the old score), an abandoned-game forfeit under Rule 4.3.9, and forfeit reversals. *Proposed:* Rule 6.1.3: "Every result is announced in the league Discord's game scores channel when it becomes final, and any later correction of a final score, forfeit ruling or reversal of a forfeit is announced there as well. The announcement reports the official record and does not itself constitute it." *Mechanism:* Trigger on_game_final_notify_discord calling public.notify_discord_game_final(); public.forfeit_abandoned_game(), public.undo_forfeit(), public.unforfeit_game() post to discord_scores_webhook (#game-scores, post-only for members). public.reopen_game_final() does not post there..
- **Rule 6.1.1** (low). Once every game scheduled in the past five days is final, a standings table (points, W-L-OTL, goal difference) is posted to the standings channel without pinging club roles. If any game is still not final when the window closes at 2:00 AM Saturday, no standings post goes out that week. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 6.1.1: "The league posts the standings in the league Discord after the last game of each regular-season week is final; the standings on the site remain the official record." *Mechanism:* netlify/functions/discord-scheduler.js weeklyStandings(): Friday 11:00 PM to Saturday 2:00 AM ET, claim 'weekly_standings', regular stage only..
- **Rule 6.2.2** (medium). A match between scheduled clubs that the importer refuses (outside its window, unlinked club, ambiguous playoff sitting) is archived and listed on the office's EA stats page, but nobody is alerted. The fixture stays unplayed until someone looks or a club reports it. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 6.2.2: "The league office is not automatically told of a game that did not import. A club whose game has not appeared on the official record by the morning after it was played shall report it to the league office with its evidence." *Mechanism:* netlify/functions/ingest-stats.js archives a refused match as 'unmatched'; bot/staff-alerts.mjs route('ea_ingest_log') returns null since v3.09; the Control Center EA stats list shows the archive..
- **Rule 7.1** (medium). Each league-office power belongs to a named staff department (Applications, Community, Draft, Media, Officiating, Operations, Statistics, Transactions); draft setup, role assignment, bans and season shape are commissioner-only. No rule lists the departments. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.1: "The league staff is organized in departments: Applications, Community, Draft, Media, Officiating, Operations, Statistics and Transactions. A member of staff acts only within the departments assigned to him, and the powers of each department are those the rules of this book confer on it; powers not so conferred are the commissioner's." *Mechanism:* CG.DEPT_CAPS and the database department checks (has_department).
- **Rule 7.1.2** (low). Only a commissioner can delete a filing; staff may resolve or deny but not delete. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.1.2: "A filing may be closed by any official entitled to rule on it, but only a commissioner may delete one." *Mechanism:* RLS action_requests ar_delete (is_commissioner() only).
- **Rule 7.1.2** (low). When discipline is issued from a case, the case is resolved and the member who filed the complaint is notified of the ruling, including the sanction and its grounds. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.1.2: "When a complaint is decided, the member who filed it is told the outcome, including any sanction imposed and its grounds." *Mechanism:* public.discipline_from_case writes 'Ruling: ...' to the case response and a public thread message; trigger on_action_status (public.notify_action_status).
- **Rule 7.1.2** (low). Every suspension and warning is posted to the private staff casework channel with the member's name, the length, the full reason and the issuing official. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.1.2: "A ruling, unlike a complaint, is announced to the league office's casework channel with its length and grounds." *Mechanism:* public.notify_suspension() posts to notify_staff_ch('casework').
- **Rule 7.1.2** (low). Staff receive a noon ping for open cases they hold, a daily briefing that pings when a case is over 48 hours old, and a Monday report. *Proposed:* No clause required. If the book is to promise a response standard: "A case left open more than forty eight (48) hours is raised with league staff daily until it is ruled on." *Mechanism:* discord-scheduler.js caseworkNudge() daily at 12:00 PM ET; pg_cron staff-briefing (public.staff_briefing) daily; weekly-staff-report (public.weekly_staff_report) Mondays.
- **Rule 7.2** (low). A commissioner or the officiating department may lift any suspension; a community moderator may lift only one he issued. The lift fails loudly if the suspension is no longer active, the member is told on the site, and the record stays on his profile as served. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.2: "A commissioner or the officiating department may lift a suspension; a community moderator may lift only one he imposed. A lifted suspension remains on the member's record." *Mechanism:* public.lift_suspension(uuid).
- **Rule 7.2.1** (high). Expulsion is a commissioner power and takes effect at once: every Owner, GM or AGM seat he holds is emptied, every roster spot in every season is deleted (his club is told), active contracts expire and signed ones are voided, he is banned from the Discord within minutes, locked out of the site, cut off from direct messages, and kept out of registration, placement and the draft. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.2: "Expulsion takes effect at once. The member is removed from every club roster and from any front-office seat, his contracts end, he is removed and barred from the league Discord and the website, and he may not register or be placed while it stands. His club is notified, and a vacated seat is filled under Rule 2.6." *Mechanism:* public.ban_player(uuid,text) (commissioner only); notify_roster_remove; discord-sync.js:1736 guild ban; CG.enforceBan lockout; RLS dm_insert; registration, placement and draft routines skip banned profiles; ingest-stats denies him.
- **Rule 7.2.1** (medium). Only a commissioner can lift a ban. Lifting restores site access and removes the Discord ban, and nothing else: no roster spot, seat or contract comes back. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.2: "Only a commissioner may reinstate an expelled member. Reinstatement restores his access to the website and the league Discord; it does not restore any roster spot, front-office seat or contract he held, and he enters the league as a new registrant under Rule 1.1." *Mechanism:* public.unban_player(uuid); discord-sync.js lifts the guild ban on the next run.
- **Rule 7.2.1** (high). A new suspension replaces any running one (and, from the Moderation card, the member's active warnings) without notice. *Proposed:* Depends on Q6; the text for each option is given there. *Mechanism:* public.suspend_player and public.discipline_from_case lift prior active rows before inserting. *Needs an answer first:* Q6.
- **Rule 7.2.1** (low). A formal warning is visible to the member, the commissioners, the officiating department, and any staff member through the history lookup; it is never public and never stops him playing. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.2.1: "A formal warning is a record, not a suspension. It is kept on the member's disciplinary history, is visible to the member and to the league office, and is not published." *Mechanism:* RLS suspensions susp_read (commissioner or self) and 'officials read discipline' (officiating); public.player_rap_sheet (any staff or commissioner).
- **Rule 7.2.1** (low). A suspension through a named season can be issued only by a commissioner, from a case, and lifts automatically once that season is complete. *Proposed:* Covered by the sentence proposed in F-7-3 ("a suspension through a named season ends when that season is complete"). *Mechanism:* public.discipline_from_case p_mode 'seasons' (commissioner only); public.flip_season_status() (cron every 10 minutes).
- **Rule 7.2.1, 7.7** (high). A Discord ban placed by a moderator on a linked member who is not banned on the site is lifted automatically on the next sync. *Proposed:* Depends on Q10; the text for each option is given there. *Mechanism:* netlify/functions/discord-sync.js:1742 to 1747 (DELETE guild ban for a linked member not banned on the site). *Needs an answer first:* Q10.
- **Rule 7.2.2** (high). A games suspension counts one game for every final of the club he was on at issue, from that club's count at the moment of issue, whether or not he would have dressed, including forfeits, playoff games and a game played earlier but imported after the ruling; it keeps counting on that club after a trade, and stops at the end of the season of issue. *Proposed:* Depends on Q5; the text for each option is given there. *Mechanism:* public.is_suspended(uuid) with team_final_count(team, season); start_game_count and team_id stored at issue. *Needs an answer first:* Q5.
- **Rule 7.2.2** (medium). A suspended manager keeps his seat but loses most roster, lineup, contract and trade powers, keeps call-ups, send-downs, draft picks and the game stats desk; a suspended official keeps all league-office powers. *Proposed:* Depends on Q4; the text for option (b) is given there. *Mechanism:* is_suspended(auth.uid()) guards in set_game_lineup, clear_game_lineup, set_team_line*, set_jersey_number, waive_player, sign_free_agent, offer_free_agent, offer_extension, respond_offer, mgmt_request_move; block_suspended_actor triggers on trades, contract_offers, contracts, roster_spots; RLS game_vetoes_write and roster_spots 'gm updates own team roster'; no guard on set_roster_squad, swap_roster_squad, draft_make_pick, the game stats desk, or the discipline and case tools. *Needs an answer first:* Q4.
- **Rule 7.2.2** (medium). A banned member, or a member with any active non-warning suspension row, cannot start a thread, reply or edit his posts; because it reads the row rather than is_suspended, the bar lasts until the suspension is lifted by hand (see F-7-3). *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.2.2 (subject to Q4): "While a suspension runs, the member may read but may not post on the league forums." *Mechanism:* public._forum_guard (called by forum_thread_create, forum_post_create, forum_post_edit, forum_thread_edit). *Needs an answer first:* Q4.
- **Rule 7.2.2** (medium). Every suspension, with its full reason text, headings and issuing official, is readable without signing in and shown on the public profile. *Proposed:* Depends on Q61; the text for each option is given there. *Mechanism:* RLS suspensions 'suspensions are league record' (anon and authenticated, mode <> 'warning'); part5a_public.js:2569 profile Discipline record. *Needs an answer first:* Q61.
- **Rule 7.6.1** (medium). While suspended, a member may file at most two appeals counted from his latest active suspension; separately, a non-staff member may have at most five open filings and file at most five in any ten minutes, appeals included. *Proposed:* Depends on Q62; for the general limit, add to Rule 7.1: "A member may have no more than five (5) open filings with the league office at one time and may file no more than five (5) in any ten (10) minutes." *Mechanism:* trigger trg_cap_suspended_appeals (public.cap_suspended_appeals); trigger trg_guard_action_flood (public.guard_action_request_flood). *Needs an answer first:* Q62.
- **Rule 7.7** (low). Any staff member or commissioner may pin, lock, remove or restore any forum thread or reply; a member may remove his own post but only the office may restore it; members may post at most 10 threads and replies in any 10 minutes; a removed post is hidden from members and its author is not notified. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 7.7: "The league office moderates the forums. Any official may lock or pin a thread and remove or restore a post; a member may remove his own post. Members are limited to ten (10) posts in any ten (10) minutes. Removal of a post is a moderation act, not a sanction, and is not appealable under Rule 7.6." *Mechanism:* public.forum_moderate, public._forum_guard, forum post and thread limits, RLS 'threads are public' and 'posts are public'.
- **Rule 8.1.1** (low). After every regular-season final, a club that no division rival can still reach on points (a rival able to tie counts as able to reach) is marked clinched, shown with a lock on the bracket and announced on the site and in Discord. The mark is never removed, even if a result is later reversed. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 8.1: "A club clinches a playoff berth when no combination of remaining results can place it outside its division's qualifying places; a club that could still be tied is not yet clinched. A clinch is announced when it occurs." *Mechanism:* public.check_playoff_clinches(uuid) via trg_playoff_clinches; app_config clinch_N_CODE; site_config clinched_N; news to Discord.
- **Rule 8.1.2 (also 0.8.1)** (low). The commissioner draws each round by hand in Control Center > Schedule. Round 1 is refused while any regular-season game is not final, and drawing it freezes the seeds (a later correction to a regular-season result does not move them); clearing round 1 unfreezes them. A later round is refused until every series of the previous round is decided. There is no separate certification step. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 8.1.2: "The league office certifies the standings by drawing the first round, which it may do only when every regular-season game has a result; each later round is drawn when every series of the round before it is decided." *Mechanism:* CG.generatePlayoffRound / CG.clearPlayoffRound (src/live/part_live.js:12257, 12390); site_config playoff_seeds_<season>.
- **Rule 8.3.1** (medium). When a club reaches four wins, the series' remaining scheduled games are deleted and a news item is posted; the final's winner is recorded as champion and the season is marked complete. The count runs only when a game first becomes final, so a later score correction, reversal or kept-score forfeit never re-decides a series, and deleted games are not restored if a final is reopened. *Proposed:* Add to Rule 8.3.1: "A series is decided when the fourth win is entered on the record. A later correction to a game of a decided series is a matter for the commissioner, who may restore the canceled games." *Mechanism:* public.trg_conclude_series() (trigger on games).
- **Rule 8.3.1** (medium). In every series the lower seed is the Home club for the two Thursday games and names the server choices under Rule 4.2.5 on that night. *Proposed:* Depends on Q33; if alternation stands: "The higher seed is the Home club for the games on Wednesday and Friday, and the lower seed for the games on Thursday." *Mechanism:* CG.generatePlayoffRound hostHigher = [true,false,true]. *Needs an answer first:* Q33.
- **Rule 8.3.1 (final)** (medium). In the final, the champion of the first-listed division (East) is always the higher seed and Home club, whatever the two clubs' records. *Proposed:* Depends on Q34; for example: "In the final the division champion with the better regular-season record, under the order of Rule 8.2, is the higher seed." *Mechanism:* CG.generatePlayoffRound: final ordered by seedRank of the division-major seed list. *Needs an answer first:* Q34.
- **Rule 8.3.2** (medium). The automatic playoff announcement and every playoff matchup page tell members that streaming is required in the playoffs; nothing records or enforces it. *Proposed:* Depends on Q35. *Mechanism:* public.announce_lifecycle() playoffs_tonight message; src/live/part5b_public2.js:632, 634. *Needs an answer first:* Q35.
- **Rule 9.1** (low). Three Stars are named for every game on the site and for every game night in the newsroom, and spotlights are published for standout lines. The two Three Stars computations use different formulas and scopes, so the site's stars and the recap's stars can name different players for the same night. *Proposed:* Append to [9.1.1]: "Three Stars and player spotlights are informal recognitions computed from the box score; they confer no award and are not subject to review." System: define Three Stars once (one formula, one scope) and have both the site and the recap read it. *Mechanism:* src/live/part_live.js:839 to 851 (per-game Three Stars on the site, linked players only: skater 3 per goal, 2 per assist, 0.15 per shot, 0.3 per plus point; goalie 0.1 per save plus shutout, win and save-percentage bonuses); public.trg_game_night_news() (Game Night Recap 'Three Stars of the Night' across the whole night, including unlinked players: skater 3 per goal, 2 per assist, 0.2 per shot, 0.2 per hit; goalie 0.25 per save plus 8 for a shutout on 8 or more shots) and Player Spotlights; ingest-stats.js postGameRecaps()..
- **Rule 9.2 (new 9.2.3)** (low). For each regular-season week whose games were all scheduled more than 12 hours earlier and that has at least one final, non-voided game, the system names a Skater of the Week (most points, then most goals, then best plus-minus) and a Goaltender of the Week (best save percentage, then most saves, having faced at least one shot; no minimum games). A week lacking either candidate is skipped and retried the next Monday. A tie on all keys is resolved arbitrarily (LIMIT 1 with no final ordering key), and there is no suspension check (see F-9-10-6). *Proposed:* [9.2.3] Players of the Week are determined each Monday directly from the official record of the preceding regular-season week and are not subject to vote. The Skater of the Week is the skater with the most points, then the most goals, then the best plus-minus; the Goaltender of the Week is the goaltender with the best save percentage, then the most saves, having faced at least one shot. A week in which either honor cannot be determined is not awarded until its record is complete. *Mechanism:* pg_cron weekly-potw '0 15 * * 1' (Monday 11:00 AM EDT, 10:00 AM EST) runs public.compute_potw(), which writes awards rows potw_skater and potw_goalie (decided_by 'auto') and a news item that notify_discord_news posts to #news..
- **Rule 2.3 (trades), end of [2.3.1]** (low). A player moved to another club keeps his jersey number unless the new club already uses it; in that case he receives the lowest number from 1 to 99 not in use there (if none is free, he keeps his old number). *Proposed:* A player who changes clubs by trade, or returns by a reversal, keeps his jersey number where it is not in use at his new club; otherwise the league assigns him the lowest number from 1 to 99 that is free there. *Mechanism:* public.move_player(p_profile uuid, p_season uuid, p_team uuid), called by accept_trade, reverse_trade and waive_player.

## Part 6. Editorial notes

- (Chapter 0) 0.1.3 (MANUAL): 'the office announces them when they are set' is done by hand. No automation posts a settings change (announce_lifecycle posts date milestones only). Accurate as written.
- (Chapter 0) 0.1.3: Season 2's row has no draft_rounds and no playoff_min_gp yet (both null). Under 0.1.3 both must be published to the clubs before the Season 2 draft on 13 February 2027.
- (Chapter 0) 0.2.1 (MANUAL): renewing Owners and seating GMs and AGMs is office and Owner work through the owner-application window and the management nominations on the Review Board. Correctly described as office work; the application window itself is unwritten (see the unwritten item for 0.2.1).
- (Chapter 0) 0.8.1 (MANUAL): 'the league office certifies the final standings' means, in practice, the commissioner generating round 1 in the Control Center. It refuses while any regular game is unplayed, then freezes the seeds in site_config (playoff_seeds_<n>), and rounds 2 and 3 read only the frozen seeds. The behavior matches; the book could say 'Seeding is fixed when the league office generates the first round from the final standings'.
- (Chapter 0) 0.9.full.1 (MANUAL, full format only): the off-season anchor (seasons.offseason_starts_at) is typed into the season editor by hand; nothing writes it from the final. Harmless while the appendix is not in force.
- (Chapter 0) 0.3.full.4 is covered by F-0-3 alongside basic 0.3.3; the same sentence in 1.1.3 needs the same change when Chapter 1 is corrected.
- (Chapter 1) 1.1.5 writes 'twenty four (24)' and 5.1.4 writes 'Twenty-four (24)'; use 'twenty-four (24)' in both. 'game-week' and 'game week' are both used for the term Rule 10.1.1 defines as 'game week'; use one spelling.
- (Chapter 1) Chapter 1 uses em dashes as punctuation in 1.1.2, 1.1.4, 1.3.3, 1.3.4 and the Appendix A paragraphs. Replacement text proposed in this section uses commas, colons and parentheses; the existing dashes should be converted when those paragraphs are next edited.
- (Chapter 1) Appendix A structure (cross-cutting): a full-format array replaces a whole section, so switching a season to full would quietly repeal format-neutral rules. In this chapter that is 1.1.full.1 and 1.1.full.4 (F-1-7); in Chapter 2 the same pattern affects 2.1.full.3 (no weekly freeze), 2.3 (trade privacy, requests, block board missing), 2.4.3 and 2.6.full.3 (fixed $3M GM/AGM pay against the season's gm_salary and agm_salary columns). Either bring each full twin up to date with v3.11, v3.21, v3.23, v3.27, v3.35, v3.36, v3.40 and v3.41, or change rulebookForFormat so format-neutral paragraphs carry over and only genuine differences are shelved.
- (Chapter 1) The absolute statements 'no registered player is left without a club' (2.8.8) and similar lines in 0.6.1 and 2.1.5 need the same qualification proposed for 1.1.1 in F-1-5 (at the conclusion of the draft; not a declined, suspended or banned registrant; not a player later waived).
- (Chapter 1) 1.1.2 'may be ruled unofficial' is MANUAL and correctly described: public.review_game_records routes unidentified and unrostered box-score lines to the officials' desk, which rules by hand, and public.withdraw_stat_credit(uuid,text) exists for the outcome. No change.
- (Chapter 1) 1.2.1 (conduct standard and its scope across in-game chat, Discord, streams and league DMs) and 1.2.2 (list of unsportsmanlike conduct) are norms enforced by people through the case system (Chapter 7 community and officiating desks, discipline_from_case, suspend_player). Note for Chapter 4: quitting a game in progress is handled on the result as a Rule 4.3 disconnect and becomes a conduct matter only if someone files.
- (Chapter 1) 1.3.1 'every rostered player is responsible for information posted there' is a norm no code checks; it depends on Q55 and Q-1-4 (settled, Part 4) for what is actually posted.
- (Chapter 1) 1.3.4 'reported to the league office': AutoMod blocking of ads and scam keywords is reconciled every sweep, but the alert destination is the rule's hand-configured action channel, which the reconciler copies from the invite rule without asserting it is a staff-only room. Having the reconciler assert the alert channel would make the sentence durable.
- (Chapter 1) The console name also appears outside management and staff views in one place: the LFG pickup bot shows a player's platform_gamertag as his lobby name when he has no EA ID on file (netlify/functions/lfg-timers.js:117 to :119, discord-interactions.js:303 to :307). Registered players always have an EA ID, so this reaches only unregistered pickup players, but it is worth knowing if 1.1.1's 'rather than publicly' is kept.
- (Chapter 2) Verification: every per-paragraph finding arrived as not-verified. The consolidator re-checked the load-bearing ones with read-only queries (waiver deadline trigger, depth draw, sign_free_agent room test, manager salary overwrite, nominee contract test, owner-app role and seat tests, trade RLS and triggers, notification functions, draft style list and gap function, RLS on rosters, contracts, action requests, registrations and draft boards, retention, column defaults) and the site strings at the cited lines. Findings not re-queried are marked in their verification field.
- (Chapter 2) Refuted in consolidation: the cross-cutting claim that waive_player has no deadline check and strands a player waived after the deadline. guard_roster_waive (BEFORE DELETE on roster_spots, trigger on_roster_waive_guard) refuses a waiver once moves_locked() is true, so 2.4.1 and the system agree; only the wording 'at any time' in 2.2.3 needs narrowing (F-2-39).
- (Chapter 2) Several fixes change sentences that rulebook tests pin (the season-settings sentences, the draft salary formula, Rule 2.7 text). The tests in tools/ must be updated in the same change, and the published rulebook updated per the standing rule.
- (Chapter 2) The camp appearance cap in the playoffs is stated two ways: 2.1.4 and 5.2.3 give camp players three games per game-week with no regular-season qualifier, while 8.3.3 allows four per series and every series sits inside a game-week. Not resolved here; settle with the chapter 5 and chapter 8 reports (add 'in the regular season' to 2.1.4 and 5.2.3, or state the camp figure in 8.3.3).
- (Chapter 2) MANUAL items that need no fix: the notification duty of a short club (2.1.2); voiding a bad-faith trade (2.3.2, 2.3.full.2) through reverse_trade and the Transactions desk; the emergency goaltender exception (2.4.1), which has no dedicated tool and records no reason (placed from the Control Center sign-up board); contract expiry, which happens at the commissioner's rollover (start_next_season) rather than automatically at the season's end date (2.5.3); the publication duty for a returning player's route into a full season (2.2.4, 2.2.full.5), whose underlying decisions (RFA match versus instant accept; draft for new registrants only) remain open on the commissioner's list; the manual-order limit (2.8.3); seating Owner and GM posts before the draft (2.8.2).
- (Chapter 2) Rule 2.6.5 (and 2.6.full.5) refers to activity and on-ice standards the commissioner sets each season; none is stored or published anywhere, so the standards in force are currently none. Either publish them with the season settings or say removal rests on the office's judgment. Inactivity escalation is known unbuilt.
- (Chapter 2) Tampering (2.2.2, 2.2.full.2) is cross-referenced to Chapter 7, which never names it; the reference rests on 7.1.1's general authority. Consider naming tampering in Chapter 7.
- (Chapter 2) Rule 2.9.3 says the draft order is prepared against published positions; the order itself does not depend on positions, only the boards and draft_fits do. A small wording change ('every club's draft board is prepared') would make it exact.
- (Chapter 2) Rule 2.1.full.1 correctly cross-references Rule 2.2 for the full format; in the full format the overflow step does favor the emptiest club, but only when an unplaced registrant exists. No change beyond F-2-36.
- (Chapter 2) The Team HQ Roster note at part6_hub.js:2008 tells management not to share salaries with players or rival clubs; its fate follows Q51 as well as F-2-9.
- (Chapter 3) The lateness incident log has never been used: public.game_incidents holds 0 rows while 7 forfeits are on file in Season 1 (DAL 4, BOS 1, NYI 1, SEA 1). Every late start went straight to a forfeit ruling. The logger (log_game_incident) is the statistics department's tool, while Rule 7.1.2 routes on-ice matters to officiating and Rule 4.3.10 says 'the league office'; the book does not say which desk records a late start. Consider naming the desk in Rule 3.2.1.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.2.4 says the league office records the outcome when the clubs waive lateness penalties, but game_incidents has no waived or served field; the only place to record it is the free-text notes of an incident row. This works but is fragile if the office ever needs to report on waivers.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.2.3 (matchmaking-trouble exemption) is applied by staff judgment: the incident ladder is computed from minutes only and has no exemption field, and nothing tallies how often a club relies on the exemption. Consistent with 'the league office rules on any dispute'.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.2.5 replays are implemented as 'reverse the forfeit and let the fixture import again' (undo_forfeit or unforfeit_game return the game to scheduled; both post 'Reversed' to #game-scores and log forfeit_reversed). The dispute itself is handled by people. No book change needed beyond F-3-8.
- (Chapter 3) The Operations desk toast after a reschedule says the clubs have been notified. That is true only in the sense that the #transactions post mentions both club roles; nothing reaches the club rooms or the site's notifications. Since v3.34 moved forfeit notices out of #transactions because a game result is not a transaction, the commissioner may prefer reschedules to go to #game-scores or the club rooms instead.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.3.1's written agreement lives entirely outside the system (Discord or ticket); only the move is audited (log_admin_action 'reschedule_game').
- (Chapter 3) Cross-cutting items routed here that belong to other chapters: Rule 4.3.9's 'unfinished' game is undefined (the system holds a sitting short of 3,600 seconds); Rule 4.3.8 puts a duty on 'captains', a role the book never defines; Rule 7.6.1 starts the appeal window when the sanction is 'posted' while Rule 7.2.2 starts it at the notice. These should be carried in the Chapter 4 and Chapter 7 reports.
- (Chapter 3) Rules 3.1.2, 3.1.full.2 and the first sentence of 3.2.1 are conduct expectations (lobby-ready, search at puck drop) that no code can check; the site supports them with the lineup readiness board and the T-30 lineup lock. No change needed.
- (Chapter 4) 4.1.4 and 4.3.5 repeat the same sentence about the twenty-minute clock display and the real-time final minute; keep it in 4.1.4 and have 4.3.5 refer to it.
- (Chapter 4) 4.2.2: code-leak detection is necessarily manual; the non-rostered-player half is machine-detected through review_game_records (regular season only, see F-4-6). Discipline under Chapter 7 has working desks.
- (Chapter 4) 4.2.4: the server list is four code literals (server_options(), default_server(), CG.SERVERS, the Discord SERVERS list) plus the rulebook. Changing it is an engineering change, not a season setting; the office should know that before promising a change.
- (Chapter 4) 4.3.2, 4.3.4, 4.3.5, 4.3.6, 4.3.7: the disconnection ladder, the other club's choice, the third-period five-minute adjustment and the early-third one-period replay are all encoded word for word in public.log_game_incident and bot/incidents.mjs; the penalties themselves are served on the ice. No change needed.
- (Chapter 4) 4.3.8: there is no incident kind for a lobby failure (the kind CHECK allows only late_start and disconnect), so a crash cannot be logged as one; a resumption from a hand-recorded score needs the 'Correct a filed result' card. Consider adding a 'lobby_failure' kind.
- (Chapter 4) 4.4.1: the only way to make a player 'sit until the issue is resolved' is a suspension, which is counted in games, not by condition; an open-ended sit would be entered as a suspension and lifted by hand. There is no lag incident kind; the review path is the 'Lag / connection manipulation' complaint subject (part_live.js:8031), which routes to officiating.
- (Chapter 4) 4.5.1 says the rule binds the regular season and playoffs; a full-format season has a pre-season, which the sentence omits. Consider 'every league game'.
- (Chapter 4) 4.5.2 and 4.5.5: announcing a ban-list change 'with that week's schedule' is a hand step; the automated weekly schedule post carries no ban-list line. 4.5.5's last sentence duplicates 4.5.2 and can be cut.
- (Chapter 4) 4.5.5: officials can verify the restart minor from the per-player PIM on the matchup page; nothing automates it, which the book does not claim.
- (Chapter 4) 4.6.2: the book does not say where a club reports a substitution. Name the venue (for example a message to the statistics desk from the club room).
- (Chapter 4) 4.6.2: public.set_game_club_substitution exists and is correctly gated to statistics staff and commissioners, but no page calls it; the one live substitution was recorded by SQL, and the importer's refusal tells staff to 'record a club substitution on the fixture' with nowhere on the site to do it. A control on the Stats manager fixture row is needed for the book's 'the office records the substitution' to be practicable.
- (Chapter 4) 4.6.2: the substitution record is readable only by statistics staff and commissioners and appears on no page, so the two clubs cannot see that it is on file. Consider a 'played under' line on the game card.
- (Chapter 4) The database view public.team_standings decides a win by goals alone and ignores forfeit_team_id. Nothing reads it today; drop it or make it honor forfeits so it cannot be picked up later with the wrong result.
- (Chapter 4) The level-merge warning in ingest-stats.js:985-987 records that 'the officials were told', but tellStaff posts to the general staff webhook, not the officiating department.
- (Chapter 5) Rule 5.1.4 has a comma splice ('naming the players whose answers are not yet in, Lineups are set day by day'); replace the comma with a period. Its second sentence repeats the reschedule duty of Rule 5.1.2 almost word for word and can be deleted.
- (Chapter 5) Define 'rostered player' in Rule 10.1 and use 'active-roster player' wherever camp is excluded (5.2.3, 5.2.4, 4.2.1); the answer to Q14 fixes which meaning 5.1 uses.
- (Chapter 5) MANUAL, correctly described or harmless: the forfeiture of Rule 5.2.1 is entered by hand by the officiating desk (public.forfeit_game after a review_game_records finding); the account a club may be asked to give under Rule 5.2.3 depends on the office noticing, since nothing records a camp player dressed over an available rostered one; the commissioner's weekly availability floor (5.1.4) is by announcement only and nothing would enforce it.
- (Chapter 5) UNVERIFIABLE norms, consistent with the system: 5.1.2 (management confirms the availability picture and seeks a reschedule under Rule 3.3 without delay), 5.1.3 (a club that dresses a player who said he could not play bears the Rule 3.2 consequence), 5.3.1 ('a game left unfiled at puck drop exposes the club under Rule 3.2' is a caution; the unwritten clause on unfiled games makes it concrete).
- (Chapter 5) Refuted as a live mismatch: the Control Center automation text 'both lineups unseal simultaneously at T-60, edits lock at T-30 ... forfeit timer arms at puck drop +15' at src/live/part7_admin.js:706 is dead code. part_live.js:10610 redefines CG.AUTOMATIONS and the automations route renders CG.admAutomationsLive (part_live.js:12457), so the commissioner never sees it. It should still be deleted in a cleanup so it cannot resurface.
- (Chapter 5) public.lineup_camp_warnings, if kept under Q26 option (a), must be rewritten rather than re-granted: it reads the sheet after filing, uses night-level availability, and still compares position groups while the lock is lifted.
- (Chapter 5) The dressed-player notification in public.set_game_lineup and the opponent notice in public._post_lock_notices both carry the stale 'right up to puck drop' window; they are included in F-5-11 and should be fixed in the same change as the site strings.
- (Chapter 6) 6.2.1 (MANUAL): "a forfeit ... is entered by the league office under Rule 3.2" is accurate for Rule 3.2 forfeits (public.forfeit_game, statistics staff, Stats Manager 'Forfeit a game'), but an abandoned game is forfeited automatically under Rule 4.3.9 (public.forfeit_abandoned_game after each ingest batch). The rewrite in F-6-9 names both paths.
- (Chapter 6) 6.2.2 (UNVERIFIABLE) and 6.3.1: clubs are told to keep screenshots or stream footage and to 'attach' evidence, but the only filing surface (the case form) takes text only. Evidence can be linked, not uploaded. Q60 option A proposes 'including or linking'.
- (Chapter 6) 6.2.2 (MANUAL): 'evidence resolves any dispute the archive cannot' is a ruling norm; the office's instruments are stats_game_set_result, forfeit_game, reopen_game_final and the merge desks.
- (Chapter 6) 6.3.1 (MANUAL): statistical rulings are final except where they impose discipline (Rule 7.6, which exists and is correctly cross-referenced). The office can correct a score or overtime flag from the site, but a line-level correction (reassign, edit or delete one player's line on a league game) has RPCs (public.stats_game_row, public.stats_game_row_delete) and no desk, so it is SQL-only today; see also F-6-15.
- (Chapter 6) 6.3.3 (MANUAL): striking manipulated statistics and referring players for discipline is an office action with tools (withdraw_stat_credit, forfeit_game, reopen_game_final, issue_suspension); the book does not claim anything automatic. Its durability gap is F-6-15.
- (Chapter 6) 6.1.2: the norming falls back to all skaters when a position group has fewer than twelve rated players (public.refresh_rating_norms). Worth one clause in the technical record rather than the book.
- (Chapter 6) Rule 4.3.3 and 6.2.1 should describe the automatic merge, the hold and the manual doors in the same words once F-6-8 and F-6-9 are settled; today each tells half of it.
- (Chapter 6) Reported by the unwritten sweep and not confirmed here: re-opening a final does not restore clinch flags already awarded or playoff games already removed by a concluded series. Worth checking before the playoffs begin on 2026-11-04.
- (Chapter 7) 7.1.1 (MANUAL): reading league channels has a key-gated read tool (GET /api/discord-ops?read=channel); footage review is by hand from whatever a filer links, since no footage store exists. Nothing to change.
- (Chapter 7) 7.2.1 and 7.7.2 (MANUAL): 'leaves the case open and escalates' has no dedicated action or status; officials escalate by reassigning with public.assign_case. A moderator ruling from the Community desk Moderation card has no case to leave open and must file or ask a commissioner. An Escalate button that assigns the case to the commissioners and pings them would make the rule concrete.
- (Chapter 7) 7.4.2 (MANUAL): the two-game baseline is the ruling official's obligation. Cases carry no rule or category, so the database cannot refuse a warning on a dangerous-contact case; if the commissioner wants it enforced, complaints need a category the ruling checks.
- (Chapter 7) 7.6.2 (MANUAL): the limit on increasing a sanction on appeal (only where the appeal reveals new misconduct) is honored by hand; discipline_from_case can issue a larger sanction on an appeal case.
- (Chapter 7) Norms with nothing to check (UNVERIFIABLE): 7.1.3 (history and harm weighed), 7.3.1 (supplemental discipline over in-game penalties; EA penalty fields are stored for stats only), 7.4.1 (definition of dangerous contact; the 10.1.2 back-reference resolves correctly), 7.5.1 second sentence (repeat offenses; reason_codes make 7.7 headings countable but nothing counts them), 7.7.2 first sentence (the moderator is not shown prior 7.7 rulings when choosing 3, 6 or 9; see F-7-15).
- (Chapter 7) The legacy lineups table and its trg_block_suspended_lineup trigger are dead (0 rows, no live writer); retire them when F-7-4 lands so the guard lives in one place.
- (Chapter 7) 7.6.1: evidence can be attached only to a follow-up message in the case thread, not to the appeal itself. Acceptable as is, but the form could say so.
- (Chapter 7) The v3.38 comment inside public.discipline_from_case (community rules 'under Rule 7.2's staff ceiling') predates the v3.39 ruling and should be updated with F-7-2.
- (Chapter 7) The Community desk copy and the v3.38 changelog promise that staff are protected from staff discipline; they become true once F-7-1 lands.
- (Chapter 8) Rule 8.1.4 and 8.1.full.4: divisions are set by the commissioner in Control Center > Clubs (teams.division, commissioner-only by RLS), which matches the book. Nothing stops a mid-season change, and an edit re-sorts the standings and projected bracket at once; the promise that assignments hold for the season is kept by the office, not the system. No change needed unless a guard is wanted.
- (Chapter 8) Rule 8.1.2 speaks of the league office 'certifying' the final standings; in practice the certification is the act of drawing round 1, which the system refuses until every regular-season game is final. The clause proposed under the unwritten rules makes this explicit.
- (Chapter 8) Rule 8.3.3: the lineup tool already refuses a fifth appearance in a series, so the forfeit arises only when a club dresses a player it did not file. F-8-7 corrects the wording so the book says the league office declares that forfeit on review.
- (Chapter 8) Rule 8.3.full.3 (not in force): the emergency goaltender sentence is vacuous in the full format because there is no games floor to be exempt from; it is harmless but could be shortened when Appendix A is next revised.
- (Chapter 8) The check_playoff_violations notice goes to the commissioners only; if the officiating department is meant to review series-cap violations (as the proposed 8.3.3 text implies), the notice should also be routed to that department's desk.
- (Chapter 9-10) 9.1.1 (MANUAL): no step publishes a season's award slate before the final week. In practice the slate is fixed in code (the award_ballots and awards category constraints and BALLOT_CATS) and shown on the Awards page from the first day of the season. Consider rewording the second sentence to "The individual awards are listed on the Awards page for the whole season" once Q39 settles whether Rookie of the Year is on it.
- (Chapter 9-10) 9.2.1 (UNVERIFIABLE): the ballot picker lists players alphabetically with a club code and no statistics, so the 'informed by the official statistical record' standard is not supported where the vote is cast. Showing each candidate's season line in the picker would help.
- (Chapter 9-10) 9.2.2: 'Statistical titles' are leaderboards only; no scoring or goaltending title is recorded as an award (the awards table has no such category). If the commissioner intends titles to be conferred hardware, a category and a season-end step are needed.
- (Chapter 9-10) 9.3.1 (MANUAL): the book correctly gives stripping to the league office, but there is no tool to vacate an award. Re-running Finalize can replace a balloted winner; vacating a balloted award, a Player of the Week or the championship requires a direct database delete by the commissioner (awards has only a public SELECT policy), and Three Stars are recomputed from the box score and cannot be stripped short of editing statistics. A commissioner-only vacate RPC with an audit entry would make the power usable.
- (Chapter 9-10) 10.2.1 (MANUAL): rulings where the book is silent are made by the commissioner in chat and folded into the next rulebook version; there is no rulings register on the site, so an interim ruling is visible only in Discord until the book is amended.
- (Chapter 9-10) 10.2.2 (MANUAL and UNVERIFIABLE): amendments ship as code deploys with a changelog entry, which fits the paragraph. Some shipped changes had retroactive effect (v3.25 recounted weekly caps for forfeits already filed; v3.49 re-rated every player on past games) without the changelog saying so expressly; future entries with retroactive effect should say so in terms, as 10.2.2 requires.
- (Chapter 9-10) compute_potw breaks a full tie arbitrarily and records a traded skater's club as max(team_id); adding a deterministic final key (for example fewer games played, then gamertag) would make the result reproducible.
- (Chapter general) The lineup-notice hole (F-general-1) slipped past tools/sql/grant-audit.sql, which the project treats as the pass/fail standard for API grants. Its first check misses SECURITY DEFINER functions that write only through PERFORM of another writer, and it treats any mention of auth.uid() as a guard even when it is only a default value. A one-time sweep of every anon-executable SECURITY DEFINER function with those two gaps closed is warranted; other helpers of the same shape may exist outside the lineup area this audit covered.
- (Chapter general) The matchup card (part5b_public2.js:630 to 636) carries lobby settings and a playoff cap as literals. F-general-2 and F-general-3 both come from that one block; reading the playoff figures from season_rules would keep it in step with the season format automatically.
- (Chapter general) Staff-facing copy in the Control Center and staff desks is where full-format and pre-v3.40 wording survives longest (F-general-4, F-general-5). A pass that greps live copy for 'pick', 'pre-season', 'room at his position' and 'three games' would catch the remainder.

## Part 7. What could not be checked

- (Chapter 0) 0.3.1: 'for the league's inaugural season registration opened at launch' is a historical statement. Season 1's row (created 2026-07-16, registration open) is consistent with it, but nothing in the system can confirm it.
- (Chapter 1) Whether the AutoMod 'Ad + scam keywords' alerts land in an office-only channel (hand-configured in Discord; not readable from the repository or database).
- (Chapter 1) Whether the automatic ten-minute timeout on the AutoMod 'Mention spam' rule is still configured (hand-configured 2026-08-08; the sweep reconciles only exemptions).
- (Chapter 1) Discord's rule that the server verification level does not apply to a member who holds a role (F-1-9) is documented platform behavior and was not tested live.
- (Chapter 1) Whether a suspension is also posted to the private #staff-casework room: notify_suspension references only discord_dms; the staff-room copy may come from another path that was not traced. It does not change Q-1-4 (settled, Part 4).
- (Chapter 1) Conduct in direct messages and streams (1.2.1) cannot be observed by any system; enforcement depends entirely on members filing.
- (Chapter 2) 2.2.2 and 2.2.full.2: the tampering prohibition is a conduct norm; the only mechanical guard is the disabled Approach button while rights are held (full format only).
- (Chapter 2) 2.3.2: whether a trade was made in bad faith is the league office's judgment.
- (Chapter 2) 2.3.3: whether a trade is a bona fide hockey transaction is the transactions department's judgment.
- (Chapter 2) 2.7.1: 'so decisions about teams and staff are made without a stake in the outcome' is a statement of purpose.
- (Chapter 2) 2.8.3: 'only where the league office has ruled that one is required' limits the manual order by a ruling the code cannot see.
- (Chapter 2) 2.9.3 and 2.9.full.3: the reasons given for where the position cutoff falls.
- (Chapter 2) 2.2.full.6: the expectation that players take the strongest offer in good faith.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.1.2 and 3.1.full.2: whether a club's full eligible lineup is online, in Discord and prepared at puck drop.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.2.1: whether the clubs begin their search at the scheduled puck-drop time; no component observes the EA lobby.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.2.3: whether lateness was caused by matchmaking trouble in the preceding game, and whether a club showed 'a clear lack of urgency'.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.2.4: whether the two clubs agreed to waive lateness penalties before the game.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.2.5: whether both clubs had a genuine connection problem, and whether either side has stream evidence in a forfeit dispute.
- (Chapter 3) Rule 3.3.1: whether a written agreement of both managements exists for a given reschedule; it is kept outside the system.
- (Chapter 4) 4.1.1: that games are EA Sports NHL 27 6v6 club games (the importer checks neither the title nor the player count).
- (Chapter 4) 4.1.2: period length, five-on-five sudden-death overtime and the absence of a shootout are EA lobby settings not present in the payload; the importer's 3600-second clock model is consistent with them.
- (Chapter 4) 4.1.3: that overtime is the same in the regular season and the playoffs (nothing distinguishes them, consistent with the rule).
- (Chapter 4) 4.1.4: the twenty-minute clock display and the real-time final minute.
- (Chapter 4) 4.3.1: that a club leaves at the next whistle after a disconnection (no whistle data in the payload).
- (Chapter 4) 4.3.8: that a club did not feign a disconnect for advantage.
- (Chapter 4) 4.4.1: that players compete on a stable connection (no latency data exists).
- (Chapter 4) 4.5.1, 4.5.3, 4.5.4: legal builds, X-Factors, pre-built loadouts and special-character classes or cosmetics (the EA payload carries no build or ability data).
- (Chapter 4) 4.5.5: that a restart was called and taken in the lobby.
- (Chapter 5) The posted guide images and PDFs in Discord were not opened; F-5-2 and F-5-12 rest on their source files in tools/guides/src, which the sweep reported were used to build the Sep 21 posts.
- (Chapter 5) Delivery of club_notices rows into each club's Discord room by bot/club-notices.mjs was not observed live; the unwritten clause on availability notes assumes the documented pipeline (memory/club-notices-v242.md).
- (Chapter 5) The count of rostered office holders who benefit from the late-marking exemption (the sweep reported ten) was not re-queried.
- (Chapter 5) The line creator RPCs (set_team_line and related) and their RLS were not re-read; that unwritten item is taken from the sweep.
- (Chapter 5) Whether the part6_hub.js strings cited in F-5-11 and F-5-12 are all on live render paths was taken from the site-copy lens (hubRoster, hubDashboard, hubLineup, hubLines, hubAvailability and hubWeekLineups render in live mode); the lines themselves were read and confirmed.
- (Chapter 5) No playoff game has been played, so the playoff gaps in F-5-4 and F-5-5 are established from code only.
- (Chapter 6) That EA's match payload carries no lobby code rests on the project's payload inventory (memory/ea-payload-fields.md); it cannot be confirmed without a live EA response.
- (Chapter 6) Whether the archive write of status 'incomplete' actually fails at runtime (F-6-11) was inferred from the check constraint and the importer code, not observed in function logs.
- (Chapter 6) Whether any commissioner has ever hand-corrected a rating (F-6-5) cannot be read back: no audit row or marker records such a write.
- (Chapter 6) The playoff paths (playoff ratings, playoff clinch and series behavior on re-open) cannot be exercised until playoff games exist in November 2026.
- (Chapter 6) The notification routing of a complaint (public.notify_action_request not reaching the statistics department) is taken from the audit evidence; the consolidator confirmed the case types and headings but did not re-read that function.
- (Chapter 7) Whether officials in fact weigh history, harm, aggravating factors and the 'exceptional circumstances' below the baseline (Rules 7.1.3, 7.4.2, 7.5.1): judgment, not code.
- (Chapter 7) Moderator actions taken inside Discord itself (timeouts, message deletions) are invisible to the site and were not audited, apart from bans (Q-7-12).
- (Chapter 7) Forfeiture of games or statistics as a disciplinary sanction (7.2.1) was not traced to a disciplinary tool in this audit; the forfeit RPCs belong to the statistics department under Chapter 3.
- (Chapter 7) ingest-stats.js authorize() (game stats desk) and the EA importers were not re-read by the consolidator; their lack of a suspension check is taken from the unwritten-rules sweep.
- (Chapter 7) Whether any suspended player has actually been dressed or played: current row counts were not queried because the database connector rate-limited the final checks.
- (Chapter 8) Rule 8.3.2 and 8.3.full.2: continuous sudden-death overtime with no shootouts is an in-game lobby setting. The site states it in copy (part5b_public2.js:629) and the Stats Manager overtime correction requires a one-goal margin, but no code can confirm the lobby setting itself. The cross-reference to Rule 4.1 is accurate.
- (Chapter 8) Rule 8.1.4: whether divisions were published with the season schedule is a matter of the office's announcement, not a system record.
- (Chapter 8) Rule 8.3.4: whether the Season 1 floor of 16 games was published to clubs before the Sep 19 draft cannot be confirmed from the database; the value is set on the season row today.
- (Chapter 9-10) 10.2.1: headings are for convenience and do not limit the text (a rule of construction).
- (Chapter 9-10) 10.2.1: rulings where the book is silent carry the force of the rulebook until amended (a norm about authority, no code counterpart).
- (Chapter 9-10) 10.2.2: amendments take effect prospectively and do not reopen completed games or rulings unless they say so (games carry no rulebook version; the changelog is prose).
- (Chapter 9-10) 9.2.1: that voters are informed by the statistical record (no code could check what a voter consulted).
- (Chapter general) Whether bot/club-notices.mjs validates the ping role id that public.club_notify stores (ping_role_id) before mentioning it in a club's Discord room; not checked, so the reach of a forged club notice (whether it can ping a role) is stated as possible, not confirmed.
- (Chapter general) Whether any client code calls public.club_notify directly through the API (relevant before revoking its grant); not searched in this consolidation.
- (Chapter general) Whether the lineup tools (set_game_lineup) refuse a fifth playoff game for a goaltender under the basic series cap of four, which would limit the practical harm of the wrong matchup caption in F-general-3; the cross-cutting lens established series_cap 4 in format_rules but this consolidation did not trace the playoff branch of set_game_lineup.
